# LAO Practice Notes — Technical Design Summaries

**Scope:** LAO Practice Notes (including APSRSE 2/94 and 2/96) from this library’s `source_md/*_CS.md`.
**Focus:** Lease GFA/SC/DDH, GBP under lease, waivers, NDA/standard-rate premium, trees/landscape, Certificate of Compliance.
**Coverage:** 96 practice notes (one section each). Missing variants named in CS but with no source in this library: **1/2025A**, **3/2025**, **5/2020A**, **12/2023A**.
**Caveat:** Advisory desk reference only. Verify the live LandsD LAO PN list and the project land grant before reliance. Individual `source_md/*_CS.md` files remain the per-instrument source; this file is the scan layer.

---

## How to use

1. Check the **supersession map** before relying on an older PN.
2. Jump via the **issue index**.
3. Open the matching `source_md/*_CS.md` / `source_reference/` PDF for citation text.

---

## Practice note summaries

<a id="pn-1-1991"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 1/1991 — Master Layout Plans
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
January 1991 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
17 January 1991 | Director of Buildings and Lands (A. G. Eason)

> Scope note: Defines **why** Conditions of Sale require a Master Layout / Concept Plan on large-scale developments and **what information** the MLP must carry. It is **not** a Buildings Ordinance plan code and does **not** set GFA, SC, or parking numbers — those stay in the Conditions of Sale.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Conditions of Sale for large-scale developments usually require submission of Master Layout / Concept Plans so Government can accept the nature and layout of the scheme, the site is developed in an orderly composite manner, and the developer can get an early indication that the general nature and proposed layout are acceptable. All such plans (Master Plan, Layout Plan, Preliminary Concept Plan, Layout and Master Plan, General Layout Plan) will be called **Master Layout Plans** in future Conditions of Sale.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. MLP contents under Conditions of Sale (paras 1–3)

Information is specified in the Conditions of Sale and will usually include:

| Item | What the MLP must show |
|---|---|
| Site / roads | Formation levels; position, widths and levels of proposed roads, emergency accesses, footbridges and pedestrian ways |
| Buildings | Nature, **height** and **GFA**; disposition, location and distribution |
| Access | Vehicular ingress and egress |
| Parking | Indication of locations for parking of vehicles |
| Open space / landscape | Communal open space; indication of landscaping and recreational facilities |
| Phasing | Stages or phases of the development |

A sample MLP is attached at Appendix I (not in this extract).

**SD takeaway:** Freeze formation levels, road/EVA/footbridge geometry, building massing (height + GFA + disposition), ingress/egress, parking locations, communal OS/landscape, and phasing on the MLP — that is the set Lands will treat as the “general nature and layout.”

---

### Source

- `9101apsr.pdf` (LAO Practice Notes for AP / Surveyors / RSE, Issue No. 1/1991, 17 January 1991)
- Read with: later **APSRSE 2/94** (12-week MLP reply; same information list) and **APSRSE 2/96** (minor vs substantial MLP amendments)
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 1-1991 (January 1991)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-apsrse-2-94"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. APSRSE 2/94 — Processing of Master Layout Plan Submissions under Leases
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
March 1994 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (R. D. Pope)

> Scope note: Sets a **12-week** District Lands Office reply clock for lease-required MLP submissions and restates the information pack. It is **not** a GBP submission note — detailed floor plans and carpark layouts are **not** required on the MLP.

---

## Regulatory Overview

A substantive reply will be given to a master layout plan submission required under the lease within **12 weeks** from the date the submission is received by the District Lands Office concerned. The information list matches Issue No. 1/1991; the sample MLP attached to that earlier note may be used for illustration.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. MLP information pack (para 2)

| Item | Required on MLP |
|---|---|
| Site / roads | Formation levels; position, widths and levels of proposed roads, emergency accesses, footbridges and pedestrian ways |
| Buildings | Nature, height and GFA; disposition, location and distribution |
| Access | Vehicular ingress and egress |
| Parking | Indication of locations for parking of vehicles |
| Open space / landscape | Communal open space; indication of landscaping and recreational facilities |
| Phasing | Stages or phases of the development |
| **Not required** | Detailed layouts such as floor plans, carpark layout |

**SD takeaway:** Keep the MLP at master-plan grain (massing, GFA, roads, access, parking *locations*, OS/landscape, phasing) — do not load it with GBP-level floor or carpark layouts.

---

### Source

- `9402apsr.doc` (Practice Note for AP / Surveyors / RSE, Issue No. APSRSE 2/94, March 1994)
- Read with: **Issue No. 1/1991** (MLP purpose + sample); **APSRSE 2/96** (when a revised MLP is needed after GBP amendments); **PN 5/2002** later shortens the MLP clock
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. APSRSE 2-94 (March 1994)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-apsrse-2-96"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. APSRSE 2/96 — Processing of Master Layout Plan Submissions under Leases — Amendment Submissions
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
September 1996 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (Atg.) (C.M. Mo)

> Scope note: Streamlines **when** a revised MLP must be lodged after building-plan amendments. It does **not** change the MLP information list or the 12-week clock in APSRSE 2/94.

---

## Regulatory Overview

APSRSE 2/94 of March 1994 already sets the MLP information pack and DLO reply time. This note adds that **minor** GBP-driven changes need no immediate MLP resubmission; a **substantial** departure from the approved MLP still requires a revised MLP **before** building plans are processed.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Minor vs substantial MLP amendments (paras 2–3)

| Situation | MLP action |
|---|---|
| Amendments **minor** in the DLO’s view (e.g. slight variations in building layout and amenity features; variations that **do not affect plot ratio and site coverage**) | **No immediate** amendment submission to the approved MLP |
| Building plans **finalised and approved** after a run of minor changes | Submit **one** revised MLP incorporating **all** approved GBP changes to the DLO for approval |
| Building plans **differ substantially** from the approved MLP | Submit a revised MLP showing **all** changes to DLO **before** processing of the building plans |

**SD takeaway:** Do not let GBP massing, PR or SC drift off the approved MLP — a substantial departure stops GBP processing until a revised MLP is in.

---

### Source

- `9602apsr.doc` (Practice Note for AP / Surveyors / RSE, Issue No. APSRSE 2/96, September 1996)
- Read with: **APSRSE 2/94** (base MLP pack + 12-week reply); **Issue No. 1/1991** (MLP purpose)
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. APSRSE 2-96 (September 1996)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-2-2000"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 2/2000 — Car Parking Requirement — Mechanical Parking System
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
February 2000 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (R.D. Pope)

> Scope note: How lease **car-parking numbers** may be met with a **mechanical** system instead of (or in addition to) conventional floors. It does **not** waive other car-parking or Conditions of Grant clauses — those still apply.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Lease conditions usually fix the number of parking spaces so occupants are served. A mechanical car-parking system may be accepted under the lease if the Director is satisfied on type, waiting area (no tail-back onto the public road), layout/elevations (including light-van-as-private-car spaces), and that provision does **not exceed** the lease minimum — with **not less than one-sixth** of all spaces in the conventional system.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Approval package for a mechanical system

| Item | Hard rule |
|---|---|
| (a) System | Type of mechanical parking system proposed |
| (b) Waiting | Waiting area + assessment that it is large enough for anticipated vehicles waiting to enter, **without tail-back onto the public road** |
| (c) Layout | Layout + relevant elevations showing the system can meet stipulated parking, including **space requirement for light vans categorised as private car** |
| (d) Quantum | Number of parking spaces provided **does not exceed the minimum** stipulated in the lease |
| Conventional share | **Always:** not less than **one-sixth** of the **total** number of spaces provided shall be in the **conventional** system |
| Other lease clauses | Car-parking proposal must still satisfy other car-parking or Conditions of Grant clauses |

**SD takeaway:** Size a conventional bank of **≥ 1/6** of total spaces, keep total spaces at the lease **minimum** (not above), and prove an on-site waiting bay that never queues onto the public street — including light-van private-car bays.

---

### 2. Waiting-area assessment report (annex)

Prime objective: **no car waits on the public street** to enter the car park. Waiting area is **inside** the car park.

| Factor | What to assess |
|---|---|
| Peak in-flow | Estimated **hourly traffic volume** at the entrance in peak hours; if mixed with conventional spaces, split volumes between the mechanical system and ordinary spaces |
| Cycle time | **Longest** time to park a car at the most remote mechanical location. Tower parks: time cycle between two cars leaving the waiting area when both arrive together and use the **same car lift / same tower**. Stackers: time to park at the **upper** space with the **lower** occupied |
| Peak wait | Average waiting period during peak hours |
| Failure | Contingency plan for **mechanical failure during peak hours** |
| Exit | A route **inside** the car park to divert traffic **back onto the public road** |

**SD takeaway:** The waiting bay and failure-diversion route are SD-critical: they must absorb peak cycle time (tower lift / stacker) without street queueing, and provide an internal escape path if the machine stops.

---

### Source

- `2k-2.doc` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 2/2000, February 2000)
- Read with: lease car-parking / loading clauses; later flexibility PN **10/2000** (±5% or 50 spaces)
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 2-2000 (February 2000)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-3-2000"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 3/2000 — Number of Houses Restrictions under Government Leases
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
April 2000 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (R.D. Pope)

> Scope note: LandsD interpretation of **“one house”** (and related phrases) for building-plan submissions where the lease or grant caps the number of houses. It is a **guide only** — each case is read on the lease as a whole. It is **not** a BO occupancy classification.

---

## Regulatory Overview

This note assists parties preparing building plans on lots restricted as to the number of houses that may be erected. The “one house” reading below applies **only** where the lease refers to “one house” **without qualification**; qualified phrases (Dwelling House / Private Dwelling House / European House) are interpreted separately.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. What counts as “one house” (paras 2–3)

| Test | LandsD reading |
|---|---|
| **One house** | A building with **one main entrance** and **one secondary entrance**, plus such **MoE** as the Buildings Ordinance requires — provided those MoE are **exit-only** and **openable only from the inside** |
| Shops on GF of a multi-storey res/comm building, each with **its own street access** | **Not** one house |
| Terrace of town houses, each with **its own separate access** | **Not** one house |
| Joint development with “one house” character **over two lots**, each lease saying the owner **shall not erect other than one house** | **Not permitted** |
| **Free-standing outbuildings** | Also **houses**. A scheme that meets the para-2 building test **plus** e.g. a free-standing **guardhouse** or **E&M room** **breaches** the one-house restriction |

**SD takeaway:** On an unqualified “one house” lot, lock a single building with one main + one secondary door (BO MoE exit-only from inside) and put guardhouse / E&M **inside** that building — a free-standing outbuilding or shop-front street doors will fail.

---

### 2. Qualified phrases (N.B.)

| Lease words | Interpretation (guide only) |
|---|---|
| **Dwelling House** | Restricts **user to residential**. Residential **flats** inside a building that meets the “one house” criteria are **acceptable** |
| **Private Dwelling House** | A house designed and constructed to be occupied as a **single residential unit** |
| **European House** | Same criteria as **“one house”** |

**SD takeaway:** Read the exact lease words before assuming flats are allowed — “Private Dwelling House” is a **single unit**; “Dwelling House” can be flats if the building still reads as one house.

---

### Source

- `2k-3.doc` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 3/2000, April 2000)
- Read with: the specific house-number / user words in the Government lease or land grant
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 3-2000 (April 2000)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-3-2000a"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 3/2000A — “House” Restrictions after Fully Profit
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
25 June 2014 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Supplements LAO PN No. 3/2000 | CFA: *Fully Profit (Asia) Ltd v Secretary for Justice* FACV 17/2012

> Scope note: Extra test for lots with a **“house” restriction** after the CFA held that “house” means the **type and characteristics of the house existing when the Government lease was entered into**. It is a **guide** — each lease is construed on its own facts. It is **not** a BO house-count rule.

## Regulatory Overview

PN 3/2000A sits on top of PN 3/2000 (one main + one secondary entrance, etc.). LandsD will also compare the proposal to the **historic house on the lot at lease execution**, especially **building height**. A proposal that does not match that historic type/characteristics is **not permitted** without **lease modification + premium + admin fee**, unless the recognition / nil-premium path in para. 5 applies.

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Historic house as the meaning of “house” (paras. 1–2)

| Rule | Lock |
|---|---|
| CFA holding | “House” = type and characteristics of the house **standing on the lot when the Government lease was entered into** |
| Extra to PN 3/2000 | Still apply PN 3/2000 entrance/MoE guidelines **plus** historic type/characteristics |
| Height | **Building height** is singled out as a characteristic that must match |
| Non-match | Not permitted under the house restriction → **modification + premium + admin fee** (unless para. 5) |

**SD takeaway:** Before SD on a “one house” / house-restriction lot, recover **what was on the lot at lease date** (height, type) — matching PN 3/2000 entrances is not enough after *Fully Profit*.

### 2. Sub-divided lots still on original Conditions (para. 3)

Where sub-lots remain on original Conditions of Grant/Sale/Exchange (no separate Government lease executed), but other sub-lots of the **same parent** have executed leases and had houses standing at that execution, LandsD looks at the **houses on those executed sub-lots** as the reference type/characteristics (houses having been erected under the original Conditions).

**SD takeaway:** On remaining Conditions land in a split parent lot, the “house” comparator may be the **sibling sub-lot’s lease-date house**, not a blank site.

### 3. When PN 3/2000 alone still governs (para. 4)

Use PN 3/2000 guidelines (not the historic-house overlay) if:

- **no house** existed on the lot at Government-lease execution; **or**
- no Government lease executed and the house was erected **only after** original Conditions — **except** the para. 3 sub-divided cases;

unless other evidence (correspondence, approved plans, pre-existing houses) establishes the context of “house”.

**SD takeaway:** Vacant-at-grant lots stay on the PN 3/2000 one-house definition unless the file shows a different historic meaning.

### 4. Recognition of a LandsD-approved redevelopment (para. 5)

If the lot (or para. 2–3 sub-lot) was **redeveloped** and LandsD **approved or raised no objection** to those building plans, LandsD may **recognise** that redevelopment’s **GFA and building height** as the permitted “house” type — **not** a waiver of the house restriction. Owner may be asked to (or may apply for) a modification spelling out that intensity + PN 3/2000 entrance requirements. Because it only records an already-approved redevelopment: **nil premium**, **admin fee** payable.

**SD takeaway:** A prior LandsD-cleared redevelopment can be the new baseline (GFA + height) via **nil-premium modification** — get that on title before the next redevelopment SD.

### Source

`2000A_3.pdf` | LAO PN 3/2000 (`2k-3.doc`) | CFA FACV 17/2012. Text from OCR of a scanned PDF.
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 3-2000A (25 June 2014)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-4-2000b"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 4/2000(B) — Recreational Facilities in Residential Developments
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
24 May 2013 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Supersedes LAO PN No. 4/2000(A)

> Scope note: Lease-administration guidelines for **indoor clubhouse / recreational facilities** that may be **GFA-exempt** under modern residential (or residential portion of composite) leases. It is **not** a BO GFA code — BA ruling is usually followed, but the Director of Lands reserves discretion. Single-residence lots get **no** exemption.

## Regulatory Overview

The PN lists commonly accepted active / passive / ancillary clubhouse uses and a **sliding-scale cap** on exempt clubhouse GFA as a percentage of total **domestic GFA** (or a maximum area, whichever is greater). Indoor facilities of appropriate scale and composition may be exempted; leases without a recreational-facilities condition need a **modification** before exemption.

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. When exemption is available (paras. 1–2, 4–5)

| Rule | Lock |
|---|---|
| Typical lease | Permits recreational facilities + ancillary for **common use** by residents and bona fide visitors |
| Indoor clubhouse | May be GFA-exempt at appropriate scale/composition |
| Active/passive/ancillary **area ratio** | Former rule (**≥50% usable area as active**) **no longer applies** |
| BA | BA ruling on facility types **usually followed** |
| Common-area condition | Exemption is because facilities are **common areas enjoyed by all residents** |
| Single residence | Lots intended as a **single residence** — **no** GFA exemption |
| Old leases | No recreational-facilities condition → **modification required** before GFA exemption |

**SD takeaway:** Put the clubhouse in **common parts** (not a private house amenity) and check the lease has a recreational-facilities SC before counting on GFA exemption.

### 2. Sliding-scale GFA cap (para. 3 + Table 1)

Clubhouse GFA including **voids, plant rooms** (incl. swimming-pool filtration), **covered walkways and covered courts** serving solely the clubhouse **must not exceed** Table 1 (max **% of total domestic GFA** or max area, **whichever is greater**). Scale is based on BA; for lease admin the % applies to **total domestic GFA of the lot**.

OCR of Table 1 (verify against PDF):

| Total domestic GFA | Max % / max exempt area (whichever greater) |
|---|---|
| Up to 25,000 m² | 5% (first band; confirm PDF) |
| 25,000–50,000 m² | 4.5% / 1,250 m² |
| 50,000–75,000 m² | 4.0% / 2,250 m² |
| 75,000–100,000 m² | 3.5% / 3,000 m² |
| 100,000–125,000 m² | 3.0% / 3,500 m² |
| Over 125,000 m² | 2.5% / 3,750 m² |

**SD takeaway:** Size the clubhouse (including voids and dedicated plant/walkways) **inside Table 1** against lot domestic GFA — oversizing kills exemption.

### 3. Appendix — typical accepted uses

| Class | Commonly accepted |
|---|---|
| **Active** | Swimming pool; multi-purpose / squash / basketball / football / tennis / badminton courts; children’s play; games room; indoor golf; table tennis; fitness / gymnasium / weights / aerobics; bowling; skating; volleyball; rock climbing; dance / yoga |
| **Passive** | Sitting / lounge; billiard; sauna / jacuzzi / spa; reading / study / library; function room; computer / video games; music / karaoke |
| **Ancillary (size-dependent)** | Clubhouse management office; reception; staff room; snack bar / mini-bar / pantry (**≤10 m²**); first-aid; kitchen (**≤15 m²**, sites **without commercial GFA**); changing / showers; lavatories; store rooms |

**SD takeaway:** Keep F&B ancillary tiny (pantry ≤10 m²; kitchen ≤15 m² and only if no commercial GFA) — commercial-scale F&B is not a GFA-free clubhouse.

### Source

`2000_4B.pdf` | BA recreational GFA practice; later LAO PNs on accountable GFA (e.g. 4/2014). Table 1 figures from OCR of a scanned PDF — confirm numbers on the original.
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 4-2000B (24 May 2013)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-10-2000"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 10/2000 — Flexibility in Parking Provisions
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
20 December 2000 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (R. D. Pope)

> Scope note: Adds a **± flexibility band** on lease parking / loading-unloading counts, and says extra spaces within that band are **not** counted toward lease GFA and SC. It is **not** a Transport Department design standard; TD advice decides whether the flexibility is written into a given lease.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Lease conditions usually require parking, loading and unloading at **fixed ratios** to GFA or unit count. Future leases may, **in addition**, allow variation of each type by **5 percent or 50 spaces, whichever is the less**, but only on **Transport Department** advice and on the merits of each case (including which types the flexibility covers).

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Flexibility band and GFA/SC (paras 2–4)

| Lock | Rule |
|---|---|
| Variation | Numbers of **each type** of parking, loading and unloading spaces may vary by **5% or 50 spaces, whichever is the less**, for that type of motor vehicle |
| Who decides | Flexibility (including **which types** it applies to) is incorporated **only on TD advice**, case by case |
| GFA / SC | Spaces provided **in addition to the fixed ratios** but **pursuant to this flexibility** are **not** taken into account for **total GFA** and **total site coverage** stipulated in the lease |
| Existing leases | If the lease has **no** such flexibility, a **modification** to incorporate it will be considered on application |

**SD takeaway:** Treat the lease ratio as the base; extra bays within **min(5%, 50 spaces) per type** can be designed without eating lease GFA/SC — but only if TD has (or will) endorse that flexibility in the lease.

---

- `2k-10.rtf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 10/2000, 20 December 2000)
- Read with: lease parking / L/UL ratio clauses; **PN 2/2000** (mechanical parking)

## LAO Practice Note No. 2/2001 — “Other Specified Uses (Business)” Zones
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
23 February 2001 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (R.D. Pope)

> Scope note: Planning relaxation of **OU(Business)** on OZPs does **not** by itself change the lease. Industrial-lease holders still need **lease modification or waiver** (and premium / waiver fee) before they can use the new planning parameters. It is **not** a TPB guideline and does **not** list Column 1/2 uses.

---

## Regulatory Overview

TPB announced guidelines on 19 January 2001 for developments in newly designated **“OU(Business)”** zones. Territory-wide land-administration policy on lease modifications is **unchanged**; after OZP amendment procedures are complete, holders of existing **industrial** government leases who wish to use the new planning parameters must apply to the relevant DLO for modification or waiver in the usual way, with premium or waiver fee.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Lease still binds; intended new user (paras 2–4)

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| Planning vs lease | Statutory planning for OU(B) may be relaxed; **current lease-modification policy and practices still apply** |
| How to take advantage | After OZP amendment procedures are complete, submit **lease modification or waiver** to the DLO in the usual manner |
| Payment | Approval is subject to **modification premium or waiver fee**, depending on the permission |
| New user clause | For modifications giving effect to these zoning changes (and for **new government sites** sold in these zones), LandsD’s intention is a **“non-residential”** user (excluding certain other specific categories as appropriate) |

**SD takeaway:** Do not design an OU(B) mixed/office scheme on an industrial lease as of right — lock the **modification/waiver** (and the intended **non-residential** user) before assuming GFA mix or street-level uses.

---

- `2001-2.rtf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 2/2001, 23 February 2001)
- Read with: TPB guidelines for **OU(Business)**; the industrial user clause of the existing lease

<a id="pn-3-2001"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 3/2001 — Premium Assessment for Exemption of Balconies from GFA and SC under JPN 1
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
26 April 2001 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Ref. LD 2/1020/00 II | Director of Lands (R.D. Pope)

> Scope note: How LandsD will **modify the lease and charge premium** so JPN 1 balconies can be excluded from **lease GFA and SC**. It does **not** set the BO/JPN 1 balcony design criteria — those stay with the Building Authority. Rates in the Appendix take effect **April 2001** and are reviewed from **1 April** each year.

---

## Regulatory Overview

JPN 1 allows qualifying balconies to be exempted from GFA and SC under the lease **subject to lease modification and premium**. For balcony-only modifications, LandsD will not entertain applications **before BD plan approval**; a modification letter plus consent letter (with demand note) is the vehicle, with a single administrative fee then **$23,000**, targeted **1–2 months**.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. When to apply and what to state (paras 2, 6–7)

| Track | Lock |
|---|---|
| Balcony exemption **only** | Apply **after BD plan approval**. State: number of flats with exempted-balcony floor space; **total exempted balcony area per flat**; **maximum permissible** exempted balcony floor space per flat under JPN 1 |
| Instrument | Modification letter allowing the Director to issue **consent letters**; consent letter + demand note issued together; admin fee **$23,000** (then current); ~**1–2 months** |
| New mods / land exchanges (change of use or other issues) | Clause inserted so the Director may issue consent letters for balcony floor space already exempted by BA; consent still needs **premium + admin fee** |
| Subsequent changes | After first consent, changes to composition or permissible exempted floor space need a **further consent** (admin fee; possibly additional premium) |
| GBP | Provide a **schedule** on plans to BD **and** LandsD: permissible vs claimed exempted balcony space |
| Auction / tender sites | New “Definition of GFA” clause will let the Director exempt BA-exempted **green and innovative** floor space at **nil premium** |

**SD takeaway:** Do not lodge a balcony-only lease mod until BD has approved the plans — the premium is locked to **flat count × exempted m² band**, so freeze balcony sizes and the JPN 1 permissible cap on the GBP schedule.

---

### 2. Premium formula (para 4)

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Inputs | Location (DLO district); number of flats with exempted balcony; **permissible** exempted floor space; **actual** exempted floor space |
| Rates | Appendix schedule; **non-negotiable**; reviewed from **1 April** each year |
| Basic sum | Premium = Σ (number of flats in a size band × premium per flat for that band) |
| Pro-rata | If **claimed** exempted area **<** JPN 1 permissible **and** permissible **> 2.59 m²**, assess **pro-rata** on the permissible-band rate |
| Rounding | Exempted balcony floor space rounded to nearest **0.1 m²** (2.54 → 2.5; 2.55 → 2.6) |
| Split lots | DLO district boundaries; lot straddling two districts → **higher** rate |
| Admin fee | **$23,000** in addition (then current) |

Worked example (HK Island; claimed = permissible): 30 flats @ 2 m² + 50 @ 4.1 m² + 20 @ 5 m² → 30×$4,100 + 50×$29,700 + 20×$38,600 = **$2,380,000**.

Pro-rata examples (HK Island, per flat): claimed 2.1 / perm 2.5 → **not** pro-rata, **$4,800**; claimed 2.1 / perm 2.6 → $15,800 × 2.1/2.6 = **$12,761.54**; claimed 2.8 / perm 4.0 → $28,700 × 2.8/4.0 = **$20,900**.

**SD takeaway:** Crossing **2.59 m² permissible** (the 2.6 m² band) roughly **doubles** the HK/KLN unit rate — keep claimed vs permissible on the same side of that cliff unless the extra area is worth the premium.

---

### 3. Appendix — standard premium per unit from April 2001 (extract)

District groups as printed: **HK/KLN** | **Sha Tin / Sai Kung** | **Tai Po / North** | **Tuen Mun / Yuen Long** | **Kwai Tsing / Tsuen Wan** | **Islands**.

| Exempted balcony m² / unit | HK/KLN | ST/SK | TP/N | TM/YL | KT/TW | Islands |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.0 | $4,100 | $2,300 | $0 | $0 | $3,000 | $0 |
| 2.5 | $7,400 | $5,100 | $360 | $0 | $6,000 | $360 |
| **2.6** | **$15,800** | **$8,400** | **$3,600** | **$1,200** | **$8,400** | **$3,600** |
| 3.0 | $19,400 | $10,900 | $5,300 | $2,500 | $10,900 | $5,300 |
| 4.0 | $28,700 | $17,400 | $9,900 | $6,100 | $17,400 | $9,900 |
| 5.0 | $38,600 | $24,500 | $15,100 | $10,400 | $24,500 | $15,100 |

Full 2.0–5.0 m² (0.1 m² steps) is in the source Appendix.

**SD takeaway:** Use the Appendix band for the **rounded** exempted m²; Tai Po/North, Tuen Mun/Yuen Long and Islands are **$0** at the small end (≤2.4 m² except TP/N and Islands pick up from 2.5).

---

### Source

- `2001-3.pdf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 3/2001, 26 April 2001)
- Read with: **Joint Practice Note No. 1**; supplementary **PN 6/2001** (when a consent letter suffices without modification)
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 3-2001 (April 2001)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-6-2001"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 6/2001 — Premium Assessment for Exemption of Balconies from GFA and SC under JPN 1 (Supplementary to PN 3/2001)
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
20 July 2001 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (Atg.) (J.S. Corrigall)

> Scope note: Supplements **PN 3/2001**. If the lease GFA definition already lets the Director exclude BA-permitted floor space, **no modification** is needed — a **consent letter** (premium + admin fee) suffices. It is **not** a substitute for JPN 1 balcony design rules.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Where the lease “Definition of GFA” already lets the Director exclude floor space the Building Authority may permit to be excluded, a **consent letter** within **1–2 months** of the owner’s application replaces the modification assumed in PN 3/2001. If that wording is missing, a modification (Appendix II sub-clause (b)(i)(II)) is still required and takes longer; apply after BA balcony exemption, because if no application has been received by **OP**, LandsD may act to protect Government’s interest.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Modification vs consent letter

| Lease position | Instrument |
|---|---|
| GFA definition includes: Director **may** exclude “any floor space which the Building Authority may permit to be excluded” | **Consent letter only** (sample Appendix I); **1–2 months** from application |
| That wording **absent** | **Modification** to add wording similar to Appendix II sub-clause **(b)(i)(II)**, **then** consent letter |
| Major modification / land exchange | Whole Appendix II GFA/SC definition clause is used; consent letter still needed (**premium + admin fee**) |
| Auction / tender sites | Extra sentence at end of (b)(i)(II): **no additional premium** for exclusion under that sub-clause — owner gets BA green/innovative GFA or SC exemption **without** premium or admin fee |
| Timing | Apply **as soon as possible after BA exemption**; letter issued only after **premium and admin fee** (where payable) are settled; no application by **OP** → LandsD may take action |

**SD takeaway:** Read the lease GFA clause first — if the BA-exclusion discretion is already there, skip modification and programme a **1–2 month** consent; if not, the modification path is the critical path before OP.

---

### 2. Consent-letter design locks (Appendix I)

| Condition | Lock |
|---|---|
| Unit count | Number of residential units with excluded balcony floor area **≤** aggregate of the Schedule’s first column |
| Area cap | Excluded balcony GFA/SC per unit is **restricted to the Schedule** (col. 3), provided max **permissible** balcony floor area (col. 2) is not exceeded |
| Permissible balcony | **4% of usable floor space** of the unit **or 5 m²**, **whichever is the less**; usable floor space as determined by BA |
| Registration | Register the letter by Memorial within **1 calendar month**; send photocopy of registered letter within **3 calendar months** |

**SD takeaway:** Size each balcony exclusion to **≤ min(4% UFS, 5 m²)** and freeze unit counts in the Schedule — the consent letter will cap both.

---

### 3. Standard GFA/SC definition (Appendix II) — SD-facing bits

| Theme | Rule |
|---|---|
| GFA | Area within external faces of external walls (or external perimeters) at each floor (including below ground), **plus** each balcony from **overall dimensions including thickness of sides** |
| Director may exclude (i)(I) | Floor space solely for parking or L/UL of motor vehicles; or occupied solely by lift / A/C / heating machinery or similar service; and space for **refuse disposal** |
| Director may exclude (i)(II) | Structure/floor space BA opines is a **balcony, corridor, lift lobby**, communal **sky garden** (res/composite) or communal **podium garden** (commercial/industrial), **acoustic fin, noise barrier, sunshade, reflector, wing wall, wind catcher or funnel** (or part) **and** excluded by BA; plus any other structure/floor space BA may exclude |
| Curtain wall (ii) | Outer face of **structural elements** may be taken as the external wall if a curtain wall forms the external face, provided the curtain wall projects **no more than 300 mm** from that outer face; Director decides what is a structural element |
| Common areas (c) | Communal sky/podium gardens and spaces under (b)(i)(I)–(II) shall, if the Director requires, be designated as **Common Areas** (lease SC or DMC) |

**SD takeaway:** For new grants/major mods, balcony and other JPN 1 features sit in (b)(i)(II); keep curtain-wall projection **≤ 300 mm** from structure if you want GFA measured to the structural face, and expect sky/podium gardens to be **common parts**.

---

### Source

- `2001-6.rtf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 6/2001, 20 July 2001)
- Read with: **PN 3/2001** (premium schedule and application timing); **Joint Practice Note No. 1**
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 6-2001 (July 2001)_CS.md`

## LAO Practice Note No. 2/2002 — Processing of Plans Submission before Completion of Land Exchange or Lease Modification
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
5 February 2002 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Ref. LD BP/24 | Director of Lands (R. D. Pope)

> Scope note: When MLP and GBP may be **enquired on or scrutinised** while a land exchange, land grant or lease modification is still in train. It does **not** authorise **formal** plan approval before the land transaction is completed.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Where a land exchange, land grant or lease modification is in train, the applicant may submit enquiry MLP/GBP after **provisional basic terms** are offered, and a whole set of Plans for scrutiny after a **binding contract and deposit**. Formal approval of the Plans is given only after the land transaction is completed.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Two gates (body)

| Stage of the land transaction | What APs may submit | What LandsD will do |
|---|---|---|
| **Provisional basic terms** offered | **Enquiry** Plans to the DLO / Specialist Section on compliance with a **particular** basic term | Comments on the **specific issue / basic term** only |
| Applicant has entered a **binding contract** and paid the required **deposit** | A **whole set** of Plans for scrutiny | Scrutiny now; **formal approval** of the Plans **after completion** of the land transaction (if all is in order) |

**SD takeaway:** Do not expect GBP/MLP **approval** on the strength of offered terms — programme enquiry comments after PBT, full scrutiny after deposit, and **formal** LandsD approval only at completion.

---

- `2002-2.rtf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 2/2002, 5 February 2002)
- Read with: later **PN 7/2006** (GBP while MLP or modification still open)

## LAO Practice Note No. 3/2002 — Enquiries from Prospective Purchasers relating to lots offered for sale by public auction or tender
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
22 February 2002 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Ref. LD 2/1020/86 (VII) | Director of Lands (R. D. Pope)

> Scope note: How LandsD handles **enquiries on Conditions of Sale** for lots on public auction/tender, including the **Application List**. It is **not** a design code and LandsD will **not** interpret Conditions of Sale, the Buildings Ordinance, or B(P)Reg for bidders.

---

## Regulatory Overview

The note clarifies Government’s (and LandsD’s) role on Conditions of Sale for lots offered by public auction or tender, including Application List sites. Enquiries go **only** to LandsD and only **questions of a general nature** will be considered — interpretation of Conditions of Sale, BO, B(P)Reg, other legislation, or site-specific matters requires independent AP / legal / other professional advice.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Enquiry channel and sale-document amendments (paras 2–5)

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| Channel | Address **all** enquiries to LandsD; **avoid direct contact** with other government departments |
| Scope | **General-nature** questions only. Interpretation of Conditions of Sale, BO, B(P)Reg, other legislation, or other **site-specific** matters → independent AP / legal / professional advice |
| Contact (this PN) | Chief Estate Surveyor/Headquarters, Lands Department, Tel. **2231 3802** |
| Fundamental ambiguity | After Application List or Conditions of Sale are published, but **before** auction date / tender close: LandsD will publicise necessary amendments in the **Gazette** and/or selected newspapers, or by circulation to the departmental mailing list — in any event **before** auction / tender close |
| Auction-day amendments | Also distributed in writing to persons registering and obtaining a **Bidding Paddle**; announced and exhibited in writing in the auction room **before** the auction starts |

**SD takeaway:** Do not treat LandsD Q&A as a design ruling on Conditions of Sale — lock SD on the gazetted/tender Conditions (plus any pre-sale Gazette/newspaper/paddle amendments) and independent AP advice.

---

### Source

- `2002-3.rtf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 3/2002, 22 February 2002)
- Read with: the Conditions of Sale / Application List for the specific lot

## LAO Practice Note No. 5/2002 — Processing Time for Master Layout Plan, General Building Plan and Landscaping Plan
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
3 June 2002 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (Patrick L C Lau)

> Scope note: Shortens LandsD **pledged processing days** for MLP, GBP and landscaping plans after centralisation of BC III MLP/GBP. It does **not** change what those submissions must contain.

---

## Regulatory Overview

After centralisation of processing of master layout / general building plans (BC III cases), pledged processing times are reduced as tabulated. Time runs from **valid receipt** (no outstanding information required from the AP) to DLO approval/disapproval.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Revised processing clocks

| Submission | Previous | Revised |
|---|---|---|
| MLP — **BC III** | within **12 weeks** (PN 2/94) | within **10 weeks** |
| MLP — **Non-BC III** | within 12 weeks | within **8 weeks** |
| GBP — **BC III** | within **15 weeks** (PN 1/94) | within **10 weeks** |
| GBP — **Non-BC III** | within 15 weeks | within **8 weeks** |
| Landscaping Plan | within **12 weeks** (PN 3/94) | within **8 weeks** |

**Non-BC III** (^) includes: Zone I straightforward cases; normal industrial/godown; normal electricity substation; hoarding plans; site formation plans; landscaping plans; drainage proposals; **NT exempted houses**.

Clock (#): from date of **valid receipt** (no outstanding AP information) to date of DLO approval/disapproval.

**SD takeaway:** Programme LandsD MLP/GBP/landscape at **10 weeks (BC III)** or **8 weeks (other)** from a **complete** submission — incomplete packs do not start the pledge.

---

<a id="pn-6-2002"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 6/2002 — Premium Assessment for Exemption of Non-structural Prefabricated External Walls and Utility Platforms from GFA and SC under JPN 2
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
3 June 2002 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (Patrick L C Lau)

> Scope note: Lease-modification / consent / premium treatment of **JPN 2** non-structural prefabricated external walls (NSPE walls) and **utility platforms**. It implements JPN 2 paras **(6)(a) and (6)(b)**. It is **not** the BO exemption criteria (those stay with BA / PNAP 13).

---

## Regulatory Overview

LandsD explains how existing vs new leases treat NSPE walls (aligned with curtain-wall GFA measurement) and utility platforms (one per residential unit, **≤ 1.5 m²**, BA exemption under JPN 2). Utility-platform consent can be applied for **before** BA plan approval; extra units over the consent count pay more premium, **no refund** if fewer are built.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Non-structural prefabricated external walls (para 2)

| Lease | GFA/SC treatment |
|---|---|
| **No** SC for “Determination of GFA in buildings with curtain wall system forming external face of building” | LandsD uses **actual external faces** of external walls. If BA-approved GFA (PNAP 13, April 2001 Revision) **exceeds** lease GFA measured that way, **modification + premium** is required to permit the additional GFA and/or SC (**including** the NSPE walls) |
| **Has** that curtain-wall GFA SC | LandsD **follows BA’s** GFA and/or SC method (including NSPE walls) |
| **New** leases with GFA and/or SC restriction | GFA/SC definition will exclude the curtain-wall system **without premium**; that exemption **also applies to NSPE walls** (treated in the same category as curtain wall for lease interpretation) |

**SD takeaway:** On old leases without a curtain-wall GFA SC, NSPE walls count to the **outer face** and will need **modification + premium** if that exceeds lease GFA — new sales get the exemption **nil-premium**.

---

### 2. Utility platforms (paras 3–5)

Subject to BA GFA exemption under JPN 2: **one** utility platform **not exceeding 1.5 m²** per residential unit, exempted from lease GFA and/or SC, as follows:

| Lease / transaction | Instrument and money |
|---|---|
| Existing lease **without** Director’s discretion to exclude BA-permitted floor space (sub-clause (b) of Definition of GFA) | **Lease modification + consent letter**; **admin fee + premium** |
| Existing lease **with** that sub-clause (b) | **Consent letter**; **admin fee + consent fee** |
| JPN 1 GFA/SC definition (environmentally friendly / innovative features) | Intended to **also cover JPN 2** features — **need not be further amended** |
| **New auction/tender** sites with GFA and/or SC restriction | Existing GFA/SC definition included; utility platform **excluded without premium** |
| **Lease modification / land exchange / PTG** with GFA and/or SC restriction | Same GFA/SC definition; utility platform excluded **subject to premium** |

Application for the consent letter **may precede** BA plan approval. State **number of units** with UPs and **GFA of each** UP. Consent states the permitted unit count.

| Quantum vs consent | Money |
|---|---|
| More units with UPs than consent | **Additional premium** for the extra units |
| Fewer units than consent | **No refund** |

Standard premium **per residential unit** (non-negotiable; reviewed annually) for **4/2002–3/2003**:

| HK/KLN | Sha Tin / Sai Kung | Tai Po / North | Tuen Mun / Yuen Long | Kwai Tsing / Tsuen Wan | Islands |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| $3,100 | $1,800 | 0 | 0 | $2,300 | 0 |

DLO district boundaries; straddling lots → **higher** rate. Sample consent: Appendix I (Director may vary). Consent also caps aggregate UP GFA per unit at **1.5 m²**; register within **1 calendar month**; photocopy within **3 calendar months**.

**SD takeaway:** One UP **≤ 1.5 m²** per flat; freeze the **unit count** in the consent (over-count costs extra; under-count is not refunded) and check whether the lease already has the BA-exclusion discretion.

---

### Source

- `2002-6.rtf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 6/2002, 3 June 2002)
- Read with: **Joint Practice Note No. 2** paras (6)(a)–(b); **PNAP 13** (April 2001 Revision); **PN 3/2001** / **6/2001** (balcony analogue); later **PN 3/2003** (NSPE wall premium rates where no curtain-wall SC)

## LAO Practice Note No. 1/2003 — Transfer of Commitment Transfer Registers and Notifications for Land Exchange Entitlements (Letters A/B)
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
24 March 2003 | Departmental Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (Patrick L.C. LAU)

> Scope note: Administrative transfer of **Letters A/B** Commitment Transfer Registers (CTRs) and Notifications from the Land Registry to LandsD from **1 April 2003**. It is **not** a building-design, GFA, or lease-user instrument.

---

## Regulatory Overview

To give Letters A/B owners a one-stop service, Land Registry maintenance and storage of CTRs and Notifications for Land Exchange Entitlements transfer to Lands Department from **1 April 2003**. Search at the seven NT Land Registries was suspended **17–31 March 2003**; from 1 April 2003 records are centralised at LandsD Headquarters for search at the CTR Unit.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Where to search and who may inspect (paras 2–4)

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| Search (from 1 April 2003) | CTR Unit, **21/F, North Point Government Offices, 333 Java Road, North Point** |
| New notifications | Send to the CTR Unit at that address for registration |
| Who may inspect | **Only** the LEE owner or authorised agent, for CTRs/Notifications registered in **that owner’s name**; copies / certified copies on payment of fees |
| Third parties | Inspection or copies considered only on **written justification**; Director may refuse |

Enquiries (this PN): Customer Relations Manager Mr. Y C LI **2231 3233**; Assistant CRM Mr. Gary Lau **2231 3290**.

**SD takeaway:** This PN does not lock schematic design — if the project relies on **Letters A/B**, search and register notifications at LandsD’s North Point CTR Unit from 1 April 2003, not the NT Land Registries.

<a id="pn-3-2003"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 3/2003 — Premium Assessment for Exemption of NSPE Walls from GFA and SC for residential developments under JPN 2
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
23 June 2003 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (Patrick L C LAU)

> Scope note: Standard **premium unit rates** (Appendix I — **not in this extract**) for lease modification to exempt **non-structural prefabricated external walls** from GFA/SC on **residential** leases that have **no** curtain-wall GFA special condition. It implements **PN 6/2002 para 2(i)**. Rates are **not** for curtain-wall schemes or the **commercial portion** of a composite development.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Where existing lease conditions have no special condition for determination of GFA in buildings with a curtain-wall external face, a lease modification (clause similar to that curtain-wall SC) is required if NSPE walls are to be exempted from GFA/SC. To speed processing and avoid premium negotiation, **non-negotiable** unit rates in Appendix I apply, subject to the conditions below; rates are reviewed annually.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. When the standard rates apply (para 2)

| Condition | Rule |
|---|---|
| Use | Premium unit rates apply to lease modification for **residential use only** |
| Plans | Application **must** be accompanied by **BA-approved** building plans. **Not entertained** before BA approval |
| Application letter | State clearly: **average flat size**; **majority thickness** of NSPE wall; **GFA of the NSPE wall exempted** |
| Design change | Material changes that may change the calculated premium → **new** lease modification, **additional premium + admin fee** |
| Rate basis | Per **m²**, depending on **flat size**, **NSPE wall thickness**, and **location** (sample calc at Appendix II — not in this extract) |
| Admin fee | **$23,000** per application (subject to annual review) |
| District | DLO boundaries; straddling lots → **higher** rate |
| Out of scope | Rates **not** applicable to **curtain wall** development or the **commercial portion** of a composite development |

**SD takeaway:** Do not lodge the NSPE-wall mod until BA plans are approved — freeze **average flat size**, **majority wall thickness**, and **exempted NSPE GFA**; keep the commercial podium off this rate table.

---

<a id="pn-4-2003"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 4/2003 — Incentive Scheme to Encourage Provision of RCHE Premises in New Private Developments
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
7 August 2003 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (Patrick L C LAU)

> Scope note: LandsD may **exempt eligible purpose-built RCHE** from **land premium** on lease modifications, land exchanges and PTGs for residential/commercial developments, if SWD supports the premises. It is **not** an SWD licensing code — SWD’s August 2003 Guidance Note still governs operations.

---

## Regulatory Overview

To encourage purpose-built residential care homes for the elderly (RCHE) in new private developments, LandsD may grant concessions exempting eligible RCHE premises from land premium on modifications, exchanges and private treaty grants for residential/commercial developments. Eligibility requires **SWD support**; concessions, if granted, are subject to the lease conditions below.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Eligibility and lease locks (paras 2–3)

| Lock | Rule |
|---|---|
| Quantum | **Only one** RCHE premises per development project; RCHE GFA **≤ 5,400 m²** |
| Alienation | **No sub-division**; assignment or subletting prohibited except as **one whole unit** |
| Standards | Must comply with **all SWD requirements** and all ordinances, by-laws or regulations in force from time to time |
| User | Throughout the lease term, managed and operated as an RCHE **to SWD’s satisfaction** |
| How locked | The above requirements are **stipulated as conditions in the Government lease** |
| Disposal / operation | Developer may **lease or sell** the completed RCHE or **operate** it; still as one whole unit |
| Vacancy / misuse | Unauthorised change of use, or leaving the RCHE vacant **> 12 months**, may trigger lease enforcement including **re-entry** and Government taking possession **without compensation** |

**SD takeaway:** If chasing the nil-premium RCHE concession, freeze **one** RCHE block/premises at **≤ 5,400 m² GFA**, unsplittable, SWD-backed, and design it so it cannot sit vacant or be converted without lease risk.

---

<a id="pn-1-2004"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 1/2004 — Site Coverage Control under the Lease Condition
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
31 March 2004 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Director of Lands (Patrick L C Lau)

> Scope note: How LandsD will process developments that use **BD PNAP 280** flexibility on **site coverage and open space** where the **lease** also restricts SC / built-over / roofed-over / covered area. It is **not** PNAP 280 itself — BA still decides the BO SC exemption; LandsD charges **premium** for the lease-side relaxation.

---

## Regulatory Overview

LandsD will process applications that follow PNAP 280 on a **two-stage** path for existing leases with SC-type restrictions (insert the Director’s BA-exclusion discretion if missing, then premium on the approved GBP). New leases will include a similar discretionary clause so the purchaser may opt into PNAP 280 SC, still paying premium and admin fee case by case.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Existing leases with SC / built-over / roofed-over / covered-area restriction (A)

**Stage 1 — flexibility clause**

| Lease already has… | Action |
|---|---|
| Discretion: Director **may exclude** floor space or structures **excluded by BA** from SC under the BO, **subject to premium and admin fee** determined by the Director | **No** lease modification |
| **No** such clause | Modify the lease to add a similar clause allowing the Director to issue **consent letters**; pay **admin fee only** at this stage |

**Stage 2 — premium after BA approval**

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| Trigger | When GBP based on the PNAP 280 SC/OS flexibility application is **approved by BD** |
| Submit to DLO | Approved GBP + letter stating clearly the **increase in SC** and other relevant PNAP 280 exemption information |
| Premium | **Case by case** on **enhancement in land value** from the SC relaxation |
| Consent | Issued after **admin fee + assessed premium** are settled |
| Later **increase** in SC | Subsequent amendment plans need Director’s approval; Director may impose conditions including **additional premium and admin fee** |
| Later **reduction** vs approved SC | **No refund** of paid premium |
| Admin fee on consents | First consent after Stage 1 modification: **no** admin fee; **further** consent letters: **fresh** admin fee |

**SD takeaway:** Programme Stage 1 modification (if the lease lacks the BA-exclusion discretion) **before** relying on PNAP 280 SC, then freeze the **approved SC** on the GBP — a later increase is a new consent; a later decrease is not refunded.

---

### 2. New leases (B) and fees (para 3)

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| New leases with SC control | Discretionary clause similar to Stage 1 will be included so the grantee/purchaser **may** develop within PNAP 280 scope, **subject to premium and admin fee** case by case |
| If using PNAP 280 | Submit **approved GBP** to DLO as in Stage 2 |
| Admin fee (this PN) | **$23,000** (subject to annual review) for **either** lease modification **or** issue of consent letter |

Consent letter (Appendix I) locks SC / built-over / roofed-over / covered area to the **BA-approved drawing number and date**. Register within **1 calendar month**; photocopy within **3 calendar months**.

**SD takeaway:** On a new grant, PNAP 280 SC is an **option with a premium**, not a free lease right — the consent will cap SC at the specific approved GBP.

---

### Source

- `2004-1e.rtf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 1/2004, 31 March 2004)
- Read with: **BD PNAP 280** (Site Coverage and Open Space Provision)
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 1-2004 (March 2004)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-4-2005"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 4/2005 — Procedure on Processing of Waiver for Ground Floor of Industrial Building
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
9 April 2005 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Ref. LD 3/1900/83 VIII | Director of Lands (Patrick L.C. LAU)

> Scope note: Waiver procedure for **ground-floor** change of use in industrial premises where the lease is restricted to **industrial** purpose. **PN 2/2003** covers **upper-floor** uses. It is **not** a TPB planning note and does **not** list acceptable GF trades.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Commercial use in an industrial building breaches an industrial-user lease and is liable to LandsD lease enforcement; the owner must apply for a change of use. This PN is for **ground floor only**; owners should apply promptly to reduce forbearance-fee exposure once enforcement has started.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Two tracks — OZP Column I vs TPB (steps 1–4)

| Step | Rule |
|---|---|
| (1) Check OZP | Is the use in contravention of the outline zoning plan? |
| (2) **Column I** permitted use | Apply to the **DLO** with a plan of **location and size** and **ownership/title** particulars. DLO verifies, consults departments, and makes an offer |
| (3) Use **contravenes** the OZP | Apply to DLO **and** to **TPB**. DLO processes **only after TPB approval**. In normal circumstances DLO offers a waiver for the TPB-approved use within **4 months** of receiving TPB’s approval |
| (4) Fees (both tracks) | **Non-refundable administrative fee** before processing; plus **waiver fee** based on the **increase in market value** from the change of use |

**SD takeaway:** For a GF industrial conversion, check OZP first — Column I goes straight to DLO; any other use waits on **TPB** before the waiver offer (about **4 months** after TPB), and both tracks pay admin fee + value-based waiver fee.

---

### Source

- `2005-4e.pdf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 4/2005, 9 April 2005)
- Read with: **PN 2/2003** (upper-floor industrial waiver procedures); the industrial user clause and the OZP
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 4-2005 (April 2005)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-4-2006"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 4/2006 — Car Parking in Residential Special Conditions
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
25 May 2006 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Ref. LD TI/1/99/03

> Scope note: New **residential parking SCs** tying spaces to **unit size bands** (HKPSG sliding scale) plus **± flexibility**, a **carparking provision schedule** on building plans, and sample dimensions / L/UL / bicycle / alienation rules. Rates in square brackets are **TD-confirmed per lot**. It is **not** a BO carpark GFA PN (see 4/2014 for accountability).

## Regulatory Overview

New leases require parking by **residential unit size**, with a plan schedule of exclusive unit GFA + apportioned residential common GFA. Developers may later seek modification (premium + admin fee) if they want a different provision. **Absence of the carparking provision schedule can cause building plans to be rejected without scrutiny.**

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Unit-size rates and flexibility (paras. 1–3, App. cl. 21, 24)

| Lock | Detail |
|---|---|
| Basis | HKPSG sliding scale — more spaces for larger units; **table in the lease**; rates **case-by-case** |
| Extra flexibility | Adjust up or down by **up to 10%** **or** **5% + 50 spaces**, **whichever is the less** (body para. 2) |
| Sample SC flexibility (cl. 24) | ±**5%** provided total change **≤50** spaces; plus a further ±**[ ]%** on residential + motorcycle spaces if inserted |
| Modification | Different provision than executed grant → apply **well before construction**; TD consult; **premium + admin fee** |

Unit-size bands in the sample SC (rates `[ ]` = TD):

| Size of each residential unit (GFA) | Provision |
|---|---|
| < 40 m² | 1 space per `[ ]` units or part |
| 40–<70 m² | 1 per `[ ]` units or part |
| 70–<100 m² | 1 per `[ ]` units or part |
| 100–<160 m² | 1 per `[ ]` units or part |
| ≥ 160 m² | 1 per `[ ]` units or part |

**SD takeaway:** Freeze **flat-size mix** early — parking quantum (and later GFA of the car park) follows the **size bands**, not a single ratio.

### 2. How “size of each residential unit” is measured (para. 4–5, cl. 21(a)(ii))

| Component | Measurement |
|---|---|
| (I) Exclusive GFA | Exterior of enclosing walls/parapet; **mid-wall** between adjoining units; include internal partitions and columns; **exclude** GFA already excluded under the lease Definition of GFA SC |
| (II) Share of Residential Common Area | Pro-rata: exclusive GFA of the unit / exclusive GFA of all units × total Residential Common Area GFA (again excluding Definition-of-GFA exclusions) |
| Check | Exclusive GFA of all units + common area GFA = total GFA in Development Conditions (or residential portion of a composite) |
| Schedule | Building plans must show (i) exclusive GFA per unit and (ii) apportioned common GFA per unit |

**SD takeaway:** The parking schedule uses **lease GFA** (after exemption rules), not saleable area — keep the same measurement method as the GFA SC.

### 3. Visitors, disabled, motorcycle, L/UL, bicycle (sample SCs)

| Item | Sample lock (TD confirms numbers) |
|---|---|
| Visitors | If **> [75]** units in a **block**: **[1 to 5]** visitor spaces **per block**, **minimum 1** |
| Disabled | From the residential pool: **≥1 per [200]** resident spaces (or part **> [100]**), **min 1**; **plus 1** from the visitor pool; location/level **Director-approved** |
| Motorcycle | `[ ]%` of spaces under cl. 21(a); round **up** |
| Dimensions | Car **2.5 × 5.0 m**, headroom **2.4 m**; disabled **3.5 × 5.0 m**, **2.4 m**; motorcycle **1.0 × 2.4 m**, headroom **2.4 m** (or as approved) |
| L/UL | **1 per [800]** residential units or part, **min 1 per block**, **adjacent to or within** each block; **houses** (detached/semi/terrace single-family) **≠ a block**; bay **3.5 × 11.0 m**, headroom **4.7 m** |
| Bicycle | **1 per [15] or [30]** units **< 70 m²** (insert if TD requires) |
| Use | No storage/display/sale of vehicles |
| GFA/SC | Spaces under 21–24 **not counted** in total GFA/SC of the development SC |
| Alienation | Residential + MC spaces assignable **only with a residential unit** (optional cap: **≤3** spaces per unit); or whole portfolio to a **wholly-owned subsidiary** |
| Common areas | Visitor, L/UL, bicycle spaces → **Common Areas** |
| Layout plan | AP-certified layout **deposited** before most transactions; no parking except as shown; alter layout only with Director consent (fee/premium) |

**SD takeaway:** Budget visitor + disabled + L/UL **inside** the common-parts plan and deposit the **car-park layout** before assignment — it becomes the lease parking drawing.

### Source

`2006-4e.pdf` | HKPSG parking | LAO PN 10/2000 (earlier ±5%/50 flexibility) | LAO PN 4/2014 (GFA accountability of car parks). Text from OCR of a scanned PDF.
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 4-2006 (25 May 2006)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-7-2006"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 7/2006 — Processing of General Building Plan Submissions
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
6 December 2006 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Ref. LD 4/1010/06 III (TC) | Immediate effect | Director of Lands (Patrick L C Lau)

> Scope note: How DLOs (and other LandsD offices handling GBPs, e.g. Land Supply and Redevelopment, Railway Development) treat **GBP amendment submissions** received while the first set is still under check, and when GBP will **not** be vetted because **MLP** or **lease modification** is still open. It is **not** a content checklist for GBP.

---

## Regulatory Overview

To streamline DLO handling of general building plans — especially amendments arriving while the first set is still under scrutiny — the arrangements below take **immediate effect**. “DLOs” includes other LandsD offices that handle building-plan submissions.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. New cases — first set vs later amendments (para 1(i))

| Stage | Practice |
|---|---|
| First set (subject to (iii) & (iv)) | DLO will try to complete a **full check** of the **first** GBP referred through the Centralized Processing System **within the pledged time**, even if amendment plans arrive during that period |
| Acknowledgement | Within **one week** of receipt of the first set, a standard letter: amendments received during this period will be **separately handled** |
| After first-set check | DLO reviews any amendment plans received; AP may be asked to **consolidate all** relevant amendments on an **updated** set (**amendments in colour**); meetings may be invited |
| **Subsequent** submissions | The set under checking is **deemed superseded** by the **latest** amendment set received; the **pledge restarts** each time a new set is received |

**SD takeaway:** Do not drip-feed GBP amendments after the first set is in — each later set **restarts** the pledge; colour-consolidate if asked.

---

### 2. Transitional, MLP-first, and incomplete modification (paras 1(ii)–(iv), 2)

| Situation | Practice |
|---|---|
| **On-going** cases not yet approved | DLO concentrates on the GBP already received (first / re-submission / major revision) **before** this PN; then (i) applies |
| MLP required under lease, **not** yet approved or accepted subject to conditions | A full GBP received **before** that will **not be vetted** |
| After MLP approval/acceptance | DLO may require AP to consolidate previous BD-approved submissions/amendments into **one full GBP** for LandsD; that consolidated full set is vetted **within the pledged time** |
| Lease modification **not completed**, but GBP must be processed | Similar: after a **binding contract** (acceptance of basic terms), DLO may require the same BD-approved consolidation; that full set is vetted within the pledge |
| Direct to LandsD | For (i), (iii) and (iv), consolidated plans may go to LandsD **direct** if **BD approval is clearly shown on each plan**; LandsD copies its approval/disapproval letter to BD |

**SD takeaway:** Do not send a full GBP to LandsD until the **MLP** is approved/accepted (if required) and, if a modification is in play, until **basic terms are accepted** — then lodge **one** BD-stamped consolidated set.

---

### Source

- `2006-7e.pdf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 7/2006, 6 December 2006)
- Read with: **PN 2/2002** (plans before completion of exchange/modification); **PN 5/2002** (pledged weeks); **APSRSE 2/96** (MLP vs GBP drift)
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 7-2006 (December 2006)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-4-2008"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 4/2008 — Measures to Avoid Encroachment onto Government Land
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
9 June 2008 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Issue No. 4/2008 | Ref. LD 7/5010/93 VII

> Scope note: AP / grantee duties to keep the development **inside the lot** so Certificate of Compliance is not delayed. Architectural or amenity **projections on or over Government land** may still be allowed if **BD and LandsD** approve. It is **not** a survey standard.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Developments contiguous with the lot boundary risk unintentional encroachment onto adjoining Government land. Government expects grantees, through the AP, to prevent encroachment throughout the development period, or to rectify it at the earliest, or C of C applications will be delayed.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. GBP, setting-out, and C of C confirmations (paras 3–6)

| Stage | Hard rule |
|---|---|
| **GBP** (BD and LandsD) | AP must ensure the proposed development will not encroach onto Government land. Provide a **block plan** plus a **table of lot-boundary co-ordinates** in line with the co-ordinates on the **sale/grant plan** provided or agreed by the **District Survey Office** |
| **Tender / setting-out** | Tender documents should require the contractor’s setting-out to be checked by an **independent competent person** **before** construction **in close proximity to the lot boundary**. AP then confirms to DLO that, having examined that report, the AP believes the works will **not** encroach (sample Appendix I) |
| **C of C** | AP submits a further confirmation that the development is built **in accordance with approved building plans** and **does not encroach**. AP may jointly sign with the contractor and the independent competent person (sample Appendix II) |
| If encroachment is found | Director may require **reinstatement**, including **demolition**. On application, Director **may** (discretionary — may elect **not** to) grant the encroached land as an **extension** under the General Condition, including **additional premium** |
| Footnote (1) | AP or employer to consider appointing qualified professionals including a **land surveyor** as needed |
| Footnote (2) | Subject to BD and LandsD approval, installation or projection such as **architectural or amenity features** may be allowed **on or over** Government land |

**SD takeaway:** Pull DSO sale/grant-plan **co-ordinates onto the GBP block plan** at SD, keep structure off the lot line unless BD/LandsD have approved a projection, and budget independent setting-out checks — encroachment can stop C of C or force demolition / a discretionary paid extension.

---

### Source

- `2008-4.pdf` (LAO Practice Note Issue No. 4/2008, 9 June 2008)
- Read with: sale/grant plan from the District Survey Office; lease General Condition on extensions
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 4-2008 (9 June 2008)_CS.md`

---

<a id="pn-2-2009"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 2/2009 — Temporary Occupation of Government Land for Building Maintenance
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
9 June 2009 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department

> Scope note: **Short-term tenancy (STT)** of adjoining vacant Government land to support **building maintenance** (scaffolding / materials storage). It is **not** a permanent land grant, not a BO hoarding permit, and not for sites needed for Government development or other temporary use.

## Regulatory Overview

Where vacant Government land next to the building is not required for permanent development or another temporary use, LandsD may grant an STT to facilitate owner-led maintenance (including BD repair-order works). Typical caps: **≤ 6 months** and **≤ 180 m²**. Apply to the DLO **≥ 1 calendar month** in advance.

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Who applies and what to lodge (paras. 1–2)

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| Applicant | **Owners’ corporation** if formed; else owners’ committee or estate manager |
| Timing | To DLO **at least one calendar month** before need |
| Support | (a) evidence maintenance contract awarded **or** about to be tendered; (b) estimated works start and date land is needed; (c) duration of occupation; (d) OC resolution **or** owners’ meeting authority to apply and execute the STT |

**SD takeaway:** For maintenance next to a Government strip, programme the STT **a month before** scaffolding — OC/owners’ resolution is a hold point.

### 2. Terms if offered (paras. 3–4)

| Term | Typical |
|---|---|
| Duration | As DLO considers reasonable for the works (body: normally **≤ 6 months**) |
| Area | Normally **≤ 180 m²** |
| Money | Admin fee + deposit **not less than $20,000**; may be **nominal rent $1** if demanded |
| Insurance | Applicant must take out **third-party insurance** covering the occupation |

**SD takeaway:** Treat the strip as a **time-boxed STT** (area, deposit, insurance) — not a site extension for design bulk.

### Source

`2009-2.pdf` | DLO STT practice. Text from OCR of a scanned PDF.
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 2-2009 (9 June 2009)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-2-2011"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 2/2011 — Standard Premium Rates for Balcony, UP and NSPE Wall
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
31 March 2011 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Rates **w.e.f. 1 April 2011** | Supplements LAO PNs 3/2001 (+ 3/2001A, 6/2001), 6/2002, 3/2003 | Supersedes rates in LAO PN 2/2010 from April 2011

> Scope note: **Non-negotiable standard premium rates** (by DLO district) for lease exemption of **balcony, utility platform (UP) and non-structural prefabricated external wall (NSPE wall)** GFA/SC under JPN 1 and 2. Rates at **date of assessment**, not date of application. Later PNs (e.g. 4/2023, 3/2024, 6/2025) **update the money** — use this CS only for the 2011 rate structure and JPN caps then in force.

## Regulatory Overview

From April 2011, Appendices I–III replace the 2/2010 rates for the **October 2004 JPN 1** / **February 2006 JPN 2** versions; Appendices IV–VI give rates for the **January 2011** JPN 1 and 2. After the January 2011 JPNs: exempt **not more than 50%** of balcony area and **50%** of UP area, **max 3 m²/flat** (balcony) and **0.75 m²/flat** (UP); NSPE wall max thickness **150 mm** under the 2011 JPN 2 (300 mm under the 2006 version).

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. JPN 2011 exemption caps used for premium (para. 3)

| Feature | Cap used with Apps. IV–VI |
|---|---|
| Balcony | ≤ **50%** of balcony area exempt; **max 3 m² per flat** |
| Utility platform | ≤ **50%** of UP area exempt; **max 0.75 m² per flat** |
| NSPE wall | Max exempt thickness **150 mm** (Jan 2011 JPN 2). **>150 mm** GFA-accountable. Under Feb 2006 JPN 2, **>300 mm** accountable |

**SD takeaway:** Size balcony/UP so the **exempt slice** is the 50% / 3 m² / 0.75 m² cap — premium is charged on that exempted area, not the full terrace.

### 2. How balcony premium is assessed (para. 3)

Same as PN 3/2001 para. 4: round exempted balcony floor space to **nearest 0.1 m²**. If claimed exempt area **<** JPN permissible, and permissible **> 1.29 m²**, premium is **pro-rata**.

Worked examples in the PN (HK Island lot):

| Case | Claimed exempt (m²) | JPN permissible (m²) | Method | Premium |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 1.1 | 1.2 | Not pro-rata | $20,900 |
| 2 | 1.1 | 1.3 | $41,500 × 1.1/1.3 | $35,115.39 |
| 3 | 1.4 | (implied 2.0) | $81,200 × 1.4/2 | $56,840 |

**SD takeaway:** Claiming less than the JPN max does **not** always cheapen premium 1:1 — below 1.29 m² permissible the PN uses a different (non-pro-rata) rate band.

### 3. Rate tables and other locks (paras. 4–5 + Apps.)

| Lock | Detail |
|---|---|
| Which rates | Those **prevailing at assessment**, irrespective of application date |
| Negotiable? | **No**; subject to review |
| Districts | Sha Tin/Sai Kung; Tai Po/North; Tuen Mun/Yuen Long; Kwai Tsing/Tsuen Wan; HK/KLN; Islands. **Straddling lots → higher rate** |
| Mosaic cladding | Mosaic/similar tile finish **not** in premium; **curtain-wall-type cladding** on structural walls is GFA-countable unless modified + premium |
| Average flat size (NSPE) | Total residential GFA / total units; bands **not exceeding / exceeding 104 m²** |
| Majority thickness | If several NSPE thicknesses on approved plans, **majority** applies |

Full dollar schedules are in Apps. I–VI of `2011_2.pdf` (OCR). Do not copy stale 2011 dollars into a live premium — check the current LAO PN chain.

**SD takeaway:** District + JPN version (2004/2006 vs Jan 2011) pick the appendix; **current** LandsD rate PN overrides these figures.

### Source

`2011_2.pdf` | JPN 1 & 2 | LAO 3/2001, 6/2001, 6/2002, 3/2003 | later rate PNs 4/2023, 3/2024, 6/2025. Text from OCR of a scanned PDF.
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 2-2011 (31 March 2011)_CS.md`
