<a id="pn-6-2022"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 6/2022 — MiC GFA/SC Exemption and BH Relaxation under JPN 8
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
17 October 2022 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Immediate effect | Admin fee **$54,800** (additional; subject to review)

> Scope note: How LandsD processes **lease modification, consent and approval** so that **MiC floor area** exempted by the BA under **JPN 8** can be excluded from lease **GFA/SC**, and how **BH** may be relaxed. It is **not** JPN 8 itself. Premium at **standard rates** (Appendix III; subject to review **not earlier than 1 April 2024**). Rates used are those **prevailing at assessment**, not at application date.

---

## Regulatory Overview

MiC floor area is the floor area within the external walls of combined MiC modules, plus associated construction joints, including wall thickness, as defined in **JPN 8 (July 2022) paragraph 6**. Whether exemption is free at GBP stage, needs a **consent letter + premium**, or needs a **lease modification then consent**, depends on which **Definition of GFA and/or SC** sub-clause is in the lease (Appendix I(a) Versions A / B / C).

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Lease path by GFA/SC clause version (para 2; App I(a))

Leases entered into after JPN 1 (2001) and JPN 2 (2002) typically allow the Director to exempt BA-exempted green/innovative features from GFA/SC. MiC is one such feature under JPN 8.

| Lease text | What LandsD does | Premium / fee |
|---|---|---|
| **Version A** — Director may exclude BA-recognised environmentally friendly/innovative features (list includes balcony, UP, sky garden, NSPEWall **≤ 150 mm**, etc.) **without** a premium sub-clause | Exemption of MiC floor area **at building-plan checking**; **no** premium or admin fee | Nil |
| **Version B** — same exclusion but **subject to payment of premium and administrative fee** determined by the Director | **No** lease modification; apply for a **consent letter** to exclude BA-exempted MiC floor area | Premium + admin fee |
| **Version C** — Director may exclude floor space the BA **may permit to be excluded** | Same as B: consent letter | Premium + admin fee |
| **No** exemption provision in the GFA/SC definition | **Lease modification** to insert the exemption, **then** consent letter | Premium + admin fee on **both** modification and consent |

**SD takeaway:** Read the GFA/SC definition **before** committing to MiC — Version **A** is a free GBP-stage exemption; B/C or a silent lease is a **premium** consent (and possibly a modification).

---

### 2. Fast track — 10 weeks (para 3; App II)

| Rule | Lock |
|---|---|
| Clock | LandsD **aims** to advise terms, premium and admin fee within **10 weeks** of a **valid** application |
| When to apply | As soon as possible **after BA has granted** MiC floor-area exemption |
| AP must supply | Certified schedule of **permissible** and **exempted** MiC floor area approved by BA (**total, domestic and non-domestic**) |
| If BH relaxation also needed | Additional AP certification of identified **MiC floor(s)** and corresponding **storey height** per **JPN 8 Appendix A** |
| Mandatory docs (GFA/SC and/or BH) | BA-approved **GBPs** per JPN 8 (exempted GFA/SC; BH info per JPN 8 App A if relevant); owner/purchaser **authorisation** if by agent; AP schedule above |
| Extra for modification | 2 copies of full land grant/modifications; location plan ~1:1000; 2 copies LR printout (historical + current); company docs / mortgagee letters / solicitor ownership list as applicable; documents certified **≤ 1 month** before submission |

**SD takeaway:** Do not file the LandsD MiC application until **BA exemption is on the GBP**, with the AP’s domestic/non-domestic MiC area schedule attached — that starts the **10-week** clock.

---

### 3. BH relaxation (para 4; App I(b))

| Lease height clause | Path | Premium |
|---|---|---|
| Height clause **with** discretionary provision (App I(b) — Director may approve another height **subject to premium and admin fee**) | **Approval letter** | Premium + admin fee |
| Height clause **without** that discretion | **Lease modification** to insert it, **then** approval letter | Premium + admin fee |

**Fast track applies to BH only if** the excessive BH **does not exceed 4% of the total storey height of MiC floors** **and** the scheme is the subject of the **planning application referred to in JPN 8**. Otherwise BH is **not** on the 10-week track.

**MiC floor (this PN):** a floor whose MiC floor area is **≥ 50%** of the total area on that floor (total area = site area covered by that floor), per JPN 8 para 10 / fn 5. **Storey height of an MiC floor:** lowest to highest level of the MiC module, including slab thickness and associated construction joints below (JPN 8 fn 4).

**SD takeaway:** Keep extra height within **4% of total MiC-floor storey height** and on the JPN 8 planning track, or BH consent leaves the 10-week MiC GFA path.

---

### 4. Premium rates (para 5; App III) — until replaced

Unit rate **per exempted MiC floor area (m²)**; exempted area **rounded up to nearest 0.1 m²**. DLO district; straddling lots → **higher** rate. If GBPs do not split domestic vs non-domestic exempted MiC area, **domestic** rate is used. LandsD picks the rate type at its discretion.

| Location of lot | Domestic | Non-domestic |
|---|---|---|
| Hong Kong / Kowloon | $10,590/m² | $6,490/m² |
| Sha Tin / Sai Kung | $6,740/m² | $4,820/m² |
| Kwai Tsing / Tsuen Wan | $6,800/m² | $4,820/m² |
| Tuen Mun / Yuen Long | $4,110/m² | $2,290/m² |
| Tai Po / North | $5,090/m² | $3,040/m² |
| Islands | $4,930/m² | $2,880/m² |

Admin fee **$54,800**. Rates non-negotiable; review **not earlier than 1 April 2024**.

**SD takeaway:** Premium = district rate × BA-exempted MiC m² (round **up** to 0.1) + **$54,800** — unlabelled MiC area is charged at the **domestic** rate.

---

### 5. Later GBP changes (paras 6–7)

| Event | Effect |
|---|---|
| Exempted MiC area and/or max eligible BH relaxation **exceeds** the consent/approval letter | Further consent/approval; admin fee + **additional premium** if appropriate |
| Later BA increase in BH and/or SC **with no increase** in exempted MiC GFA area | Additional premium **may not** be charged |
| Floor no longer qualifies as MiC floor (**< 50%** MiC area) **or** storey height of an MiC floor is **reduced** | BH relaxation under lease becomes **invalid** |
| MiC not adopted / BA GFA/SC concession revoked | Premium and admin fee **non-refundable** |
| Live land exchange / grant / modification | Applicant may ask for **Version A** GFA/SC text so later MiC (and other green-feature) exemptions need **no** consent letter |

**SD takeaway:** Do not drop an MiC floor below **50%** MiC area or shave its module height after consent — the BH relaxation **falls away**, and premium already paid is **kept**.

---

### Source

- `PN 6_2022.pdf`
- Read with: **JPN 8** (July 2022); **JPN 1 / JPN 2** (green-feature GFA/SC sub-clause)
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 6-2022 (October 2022)_CS.md`

---

<a id="pn-2-2023"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 2/2023 — Processing lease modifications, exchanges, PTGs and other land transactions
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
March 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Immediate effect | Supersedes LAO PN Nos. **3/1999** (APSS 2/99), **9/2000**, **4/2001**, **1/2006**, **5/2006**, **9/2006**, **4/2007**, **5/2007**, **3/2008**, **3/2009** and **4/2009**

> Scope note: Territory-wide **procedure** for lease modification, land exchange, private treaty grant and other land transactions. **Does not cover** Small House Policy or **temporary** transactions. NDA cases (e.g. PN 1/2022) may use modified procedures.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Applications are accepted only from the registered owner, a prospective purchaser under a **registered ASP** with the owner’s written consent, or their authorised representative. For exchanges, the applicant (including wholly-owned subsidiaries) must already own **all** lots to be surrendered or be such a prospective purchaser. LandsD acts as landlord and may approve or reject on the merits.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Who applies and first pack (para 2(i), App II)

| Must submit | Facilitate |
|---|---|
| Letter identifying property and sought variations (**user, max GFA, SC, storeys/BH**, etc.) | OZP extract + notes |
| 1:1000 location/site plan | Assignment / Deed Poll plans for portions |
| TPB approval letter if applicable | Sketch plans if available |
| Ownership printout certified by LR or solicitor, **≤ 1 month** old (hard or soft copy) | Approved MLP / EIA / TIA / DIA etc. |
| Agent authorisation / purchaser’s owner consent | |

**SD takeaway:** The opening letter must already state the **GFA / SC / BH / user** ask — that is what DLC will see.

---

### 2. Fees, title, DLC (para 2(ii)–(iv))

| Stage | Lock |
|---|---|
| Valid for processing | Demand notes for **initial administrative fee** and, if surrender is involved, **initial LACO fee**; unpaid by the stated date → **deemed withdrawn** |
| Exchange title | Submit title deeds; rectify defects, clear encumbrances (e.g. outstanding premium instalments), prove good title within the specified period (App IV) |
| DLC | Applicant may attend (confirm **≥ 2 working days** ahead) or write in; papers confidential and **not binding** |

**SD takeaway:** Budget initial fees immediately after validation; for exchanges, title unification is on the same critical path as design.

---

### 3. PBTO → premium → BBTO → execution (para 2(v)–(x), App I time targets)

| Step | Clock / money |
|---|---|
| Preliminary reply (pledge) | **3 weeks** — can the application be processed? |
| Reject / PBTO / in-principle (pledge) | **22 weeks** from preliminary reply |
| Pay initial fees / accept PBTO | Generally **2 weeks** (date in the letter) |
| PBTO missed | Deemed withdrawal; fees **forfeited** |
| Nil or nominal premium (e.g. $1,000) | Usually a **single** offer; two-stage optional (para 3) |
| BBTO accept | Generally **4 weeks**: execute Acceptance Letter; pay **balance** admin + LACO fees; pay **10% premium deposit**; mortgagee no-objection letter |
| Contract Date | Date LandsD **receives** acceptance |
| Execute documents | On or before a date **within 12 weeks of Contract Date**; pay balance premium + Land Registry fees. Failure = breach; fees **and deposit forfeited** |
| Appeal instead of accept BBTO | Signed Appeal Letter (basic terms other than premium accepted) + settle fee balances by BBTO date (App V) |
| Same-amount revised premium then refused | Deemed withdrawal; fees forfeited |
| Due date on Sat/PH or Black Rain / T8+ / “extreme conditions” | Next working day (para 4) |

**SD takeaway:** From PBTO acceptance you are on a **premium + 12-week execution** rail — freeze design before PBTO; 10% deposit is due at BBTO, not at execution.

---

### 4. Exchange title after Contract Date (App IV)

Within **8 weeks of Contract Date** (as applicable): discharge mortgages (draft release to LACO); **unify title** and register; rectify remaining title defects; complete ASP assignment and register.

**SD takeaway:** Multi-owner or mortgaged lots must be cleared inside 8 weeks of BBTO acceptance or the 12-week execution date is at risk.

---

### 5. Premium assessment and appeal (App V) — design-facing bits only

| Item | Lock |
|---|---|
| After PBTO accept | Applicant may send valuation-useful info within **2 weeks**; **no negotiation** on the first assessment |
| Appeal | Returning Appeal Letter **lapses** the offered premium; re-assessment is at **then** market (can go up or down) |
| Counter-offer | With supporting info within **3 months** of Appeal Letter |
| Fast track (2nd and 3rd appeals only) | Appeal Letter + justified counter-offer within **12 working days** of revised BBTO, copied to Valuation Section; if qualified, revised BBTO within **24 working days**. No justifications / recycled justifications / policy-legal follow-up / **basic-term amendments** → not qualified. **4th+ appeals**: fast track **unavailable** |

**SD takeaway:** Changing GFA/user after BBTO is a **basic-terms** change and kills fast-track premium appeal — lock the scheme before appealing numbers.

---

### Source

- `PN 2_2023.pdf`
- NDA / special cases may follow **LAO PN No. 1/2022** (and later NDA PNs) instead of this flow
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 2-2023 (March 2023)_CS.md`

---

<a id="pn-3-2023"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 3/2023 — Offensive trades licences for specified food businesses
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
March 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Supersedes LAO PN No. 3/2021 as varied by PN No. 1/2023

> Scope note: How old-lease **offensive trades** clauses interact with **hotel/food** users. Streamlines **deemed OT Licences** for eleven FEHD/LLB food-and-liquor licences. It is **not** a BO use code, **not** a DMC waiver, and **does not** permit hotel via the deemed route.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Many old Government leases ban named trades (including sugar-baker, oilman, butcher, victualler, tavern-keeper — “the five trades”) without an OT Licence. From the date of this PN, specified statutory food/liquor licences can **deem** an OT Licence when three criteria are met; applied-for licences remain available for whole-lot and unit cases.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. OT Licence on application — whole lot (paras 2–3, 5–6)

| Item | Lock |
|---|---|
| Five trades unlocked | Sugar baker, oilman, butcher, victualler, tavern-keeper |
| **Hotel** (tavern-keeper lifted) | OT Licence will **restrict sale of any hotel except as a whole** and **letting of hotel rooms for not more than one year**; **delineate hotel portion on building plans** |
| Fee | **$32,600 per lot** (subject to review); LandsD aims to offer within **two months** for straightforward cases |
| Title printout | LR- or solicitor-certified, **not more than one month** old; hard or pdf |

**SD takeaway:** A hotel on an old OT-clause lot needs an **applied-for** OT Licence plus a **plan-delineated hotel portion** and whole-building sale / ≤1-year letting locks — not the deemed-licence stream.

---

### 2. Multi-ownership / single units (para 4)

| Item | Lock |
|---|---|
| Coverage | One or more units; registered owner of the premises |
| Hotel | OT Licence will **prohibit hotel use** of those premises |
| Fee | Same administrative fee as para 3(ii) |

**SD takeaway:** Unit-level F&B can be licensed; do not draw a hotel user on a unit OT Licence.

---

### 3. Deemed OT Licence for eleven statutory licences (paras 7–9)

Takes effect from this PN’s date. Deemed grant to the **owner** when **all three Criteria** are met:

| Criterion | Lock |
|---|---|
| (a) | Lot/premises **not** already covered by an OT Licence |
| (b) | Proposed use falls within one or more of the **five trades** |
| (c) | Lot/premises is **not part of a hotel development** and remains so during the statutory licence term |

**Statutory Licences (any one or more):**

| FEHD / LLB licence |
|---|
| General Restaurant; Light Refreshment Restaurant; Bakery; Food Factory; Fresh Provision Shop; Frozen Confection Factory; Milk Factory; Siu Mei and Lo Mei Shop; Composite Food Shop; Liquor Licence; Club Liquor Licence |

| Term | Lock |
|---|---|
| Duration | **Co-terminus** with the relevant Statutory Licence(s) |
| Auto-end | When all such licences expire/cancel, **or** any Criterion fails → full offensive-trades clause revives |

**SD takeaway:** Restaurant/bakery/FPS (etc.) on a non-hotel old-lease lot generally needs **no separate LandsD OT application** once the FEHD/LLB licence is granted; hotel and mixed hotel-podium F&B still need the applied-for route.

---

### 4. What an OT Licence does **not** override (paras 6, 12)

OT Licence (issued or deemed) does not displace other lease covenants, the Buildings Ordinance, the Town Planning Ordinance, other legislation, or **DMC** rights.

**SD takeaway:** Check OZP user, DMC, and BO restaurant fit-out independently of the OT stream.

---

### Source

- `PN 3_2023.pdf`
- Supersedes: **LAO PN No. 6/2007**; **PN 3/2021** as varied by **PN 1/2023**
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 3-2023 (March 2023)_CS.md`

---

<a id="pn-4-2023"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 4/2023 — Standard Rates for JPN 1 & 2 Balcony / UP / NSPEWall Premium
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
31 March 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Rates effective **1 April 2023** | Admin fee **$54,800** (additional, non-negotiable, subject to review)

> Scope note: **Rate revision only** for premium on lease exemption of balconies, utility platforms and non-structural prefabricated external walls from GFA and SC under **JPN 1** and **JPN 2**. Varies/supplements LAO PN **3/2001** (as supplemented by **6/2001**), **6/2002**, **3/2003** and **2/2011**; **supersedes** PN **3/2001A** and **1/2018**. It does **not** restate JPN dimensional rules.

---

## Regulatory Overview

From **1 April 2023**, after annual review, the standard premium rates for those JPN 1/2 exemptions are revised **downwards**. Which appendix applies depends on the **JPN version** on the lease (October 2004 vs January 2011 JPN 1; February 2006 vs January 2011 JPN 2, or later variations).

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Which table / which JPN vintage (paras 2–3)

| Feature | JPN vintage | Appendix |
|---|---|---|
| Balcony GFA/SC exemption | JPN 1 **October 2004** | **I** — premium **per unit** by total exempted balcony m² (2.0–5.0) |
| Balcony GFA/SC exemption | JPN 1 **January 2011** | **II** — premium **per unit** by total exempted balcony m² (1.0–3.0) |
| Utility platform | JPN 2 **February 2006** | **III** — **flat** premium per residential unit |
| Utility platform | JPN 2 **January 2011** | **IV** — **flat** premium per residential unit |
| NSPEWall | JPN 2 **February 2006** | **V** — $/m² of BA-approved exempted area; thickness **≤ 300 mm** (excess **GFA-accountable**) |
| NSPEWall | JPN 2 **January 2011** | **VI** — same method; thickness **> 150 mm** is **GFA-accountable** |

| Cross-cutting rule | Lock |
|---|---|
| Admin fee | **$54,800** on top of premium |
| Rates + fee | **Non-negotiable**; subject to review |
| District | Boundary of the **District Lands Office**; lot straddling two districts → **higher** rate |
| Balcony rounding | AP measures to nearest **0.001 m²**; **2.544 m² → 2.5 m²**; **2.545 m² → 2.6 m²** |

**SD takeaway:** Read the lease’s JPN 1/2 vintage first — 2011 tables are a different (generally lower) schedule and a tighter NSPEWall thickness cap (**150 mm** vs **300 mm**).

---

### 2. Balcony — representative per-unit premiums from 1.4.2023 (App I–II)

**October 2004 JPN 1 (App I)** — premium per unit (HK$):

| Exempted balcony m²/unit | HK/Kowloon | Sha Tin/Sai Kung | Tai Po/North | Tuen Mun/Yuen Long | Kwai Tsing/Tsuen Wan | Islands |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.0 | 97,400 | 59,300 | 35,900 | 27,100 | 59,700 | 34,700 |
| 3.0 | 186,400 | 85,400 | 54,000 | 40,200 | 77,100 | 51,800 |
| 5.0 | 418,800 | 150,400 | 98,700 | 75,800 | 131,300 | 95,100 |

**January 2011 JPN 1 (App II)** — premium per unit (HK$):

| Exempted balcony m²/unit | HK/Kowloon | Sha Tin/Sai Kung | Tai Po/North | Tuen Mun/Yuen Long | Kwai Tsing/Tsuen Wan | Islands |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.0 | 32,000 | 15,700 | 4,200 | 1,400 | 15,900 | 3,600 |
| 2.0 | 103,800 | 47,000 | 29,700 | 22,000 | 40,700 | 28,100 |
| 3.0 | 203,200 | 75,100 | 49,200 | 37,800 | 65,800 | 46,700 |

Full 0.1 m² steps are in the appendices (App I: 2.0–5.0; App II: 1.0–3.0).

**SD takeaway:** Budget balcony premium **per flat** from the rounded exempted area and DLO district — HK/Kowloon at 5.0 m² is **$418,800**/unit on the 2004 table.

---

### 3. Utility platform — standard premium per residential unit from 1.4.2023 (App III–IV)

| JPN 2 version | HK/Kowloon | Sha Tin/Sai Kung | Tai Po/North | Tuen Mun/Yuen Long | Kwai Tsing/Tsuen Wan | Islands |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Feb 2006** | $73,050 | $44,480 | $26,930 | $20,330 | $44,780 | $26,030 |
| **Jan 2011** | $24,000 | $11,780 | $3,150 | $1,050 | $11,930 | $2,700 |

**SD takeaway:** UP premium is a **flat per-unit** ticket, not area-banded — 2011 vintage is roughly one-third of 2006 in HK/Kowloon.

---

### 4. NSPEWall — $/m² of BA-exempted area from 1.4.2023 (App V–VI)

Thickness is as shown on **approved building plans**; if more than one thickness, **majority** principle. Average flat size = total residential GFA / total residential units; bands split at **104 m²(G)**. Mosaic/similar **tile cladding** that only beautifies is **not** in the premium calc; **curtain-wall-type** cladding on structural walls is GFA-countable unless exempted by lease modification + assessed premium.

**130 mm or less** (both App V Feb 2006 and App VI Jan 2011 share this band), HK$/m²:

| District | ≤ 104 m²(G) | > 104 m²(G) |
|---|---|---|
| Hong Kong / Kowloon | 103,220 | 158,820 |
| Sha Tin / Sai Kung | 67,290 | 64,440 |
| Tai Po / North | 45,150 | 43,000 |
| Tuen Mun / Yuen Long | 37,490 | 33,600 |
| Kwai Tsing / Tsuen Wan | 67,680 | 56,520 |
| Islands | 44,900 | 41,580 |

App V continues in 10 mm steps to **290–300 mm**. App VI (Jan 2011) stops at **140–150 mm** because thickness **> 150 mm** is GFA-accountable under that JPN 2.

**SD takeaway:** Keep NSPEWall **≤ 150 mm** on 2011 leases (or **≤ 300 mm** on 2006) or the excess is **GFA**, not a premium exemption — and do not count decorative tile cladding in the premium area.

---

### Source

- `PN 4_2023.pdf`
- Read with: **JPN 1** / **JPN 2** (the version cited in the lease); parent rate PNs **3/2001** (as supplemented by **6/2001**), **6/2002**, **3/2003**, **2/2011** (superseded: **3/2001A**, **1/2018**)

<a id="pn-5-2023"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 5/2023 — RCHE Premises Incentive (Time-limited Enhancements)
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
20 June 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Pilot: **20 June 2023 – 19 June 2026** | During the Pilot, **LAO PN 4/2003** is **not** applicable

> Scope note: Time-limited **premium and lease-GFA** concessions to put **licensed RCHE** in **new** private developments (not industrial; not A&A/conversion of existing buildings). It is **not** a Cap. 459 licensing manual. SWD support is mandatory. Read SWD’s June 2023 Guidance Note.

---

## Regulatory Overview

During the three-year Pilot, LandsD may (i) **exempt eligible RCHE from land premium** on lease modification, land exchange or PTG for a new private development, and (ii) **exempt eligible RCHE from total permissible GFA under lease**. Applications received after the Pilot will be processed under the parameters of the Incentive Scheme (and **PN 4/2003** is not applicable during the Pilot).

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. What is “new” and what BA/TPB will do (paras 1–2 and footnotes)

| Rule | Lock |
|---|---|
| New private development | **Excludes industrial** developments; **addition, alteration or conversion of existing building(s)** is **not** a new private development |
| Planning | Must comply with **TPO**; s.12A or s.16 if RCHE is not always permitted and/or **exceeds** OZP PR/GFA/BH |
| BO GFA | RCHE in private development is **GFA-accountable** under B(P)R |
| BA during Pilot | Prepared to grant **modification** for **higher PR/GFA** to accommodate the **additional RCHE GFA**, taking PlanD’s advice on OZP/planning-approval compliance when processing GBP |

**SD takeaway:** Only a **new** (non-industrial) scheme gets this; RCHE GFA is still BO-accountable unless BA grants the Pilot **PR/GFA modification** — start TPB if the OZP envelope is exceeded.

---

### 2. Concession cap and lease locks (para 3)

| Condition | Hard limit |
|---|---|
| SWD | Provision **must** have SWD support; **size and location of each** RCHE approved by SWD |
| GFA cap | **≤ 12,000 m²** total RCHE GFA **or 10%** of total permissible GFA under lease, **whichever is the greater** |
| How many RCHEs | **More than one** eligible RCHE per site may take the Concessions |
| Alienation | **No sub-division**; assignment, mortgage, underletting or other disposal **only as a whole unit** |
| Operation | Throughout the lease term, managed/operated as **licensed RCHE(s)** under Cap. **459** and subsidiary legislation, to SWD’s satisfaction |
| Lease | Above requirements **stipulated as lease conditions** as appropriate |
| After completion (para 4) | Developer may **lease or sell each RCHE as a whole unit** or **operate** it; unauthorised change of use or vacancy **> 12 months** (as identified by SWD) may trigger lease enforcement including **re-entry** and Government taking possession **without compensation** |

**SD takeaway:** Size the RCHE package to **max(12,000 m², 10% of lease GFA)**, get **SWD** to sign off location/size, and keep each RCHE as one **unsplittable** whole-floor (or whole-premises) unit — vacancy over **12 months** is an enforcement risk.

---

### 3. How RCHE GFA is measured for this PN (Annex)

**Included:** (a) RCHE(s) including **enclosing walls** and structures within the premises; (b) staircases, lift shafts, lobbies and corridors **solely serving** the RCHE(s), including horizontal areas of those stairs/lifts **passing through the RCHE floor** and serving RCHE **only** at that floor.

**Not included:** horizontal areas of stairs, lifts, lobbies and corridors **shared with other uses on the same floor**.

| Remark | Lock |
|---|---|
| Cap. 459 licensing floor space | Enclosing walls and internal structures are **excluded** from area-per-resident (different from this GFA) |
| Plant / parking / LUL | May be disregarded under the prevailing GFA-concession mechanism **except** remark (4) |
| Non-mandatory/non-essential plant rooms and vertical-duct horizontal areas **solely serving** RCHE | **Not** subject to the **10%** overall GFA-concession cap under **PNAP APP-151** |
| Authority | PlanD and LandsD rely on **BD’s verified GFA** on the GBP |
| Case-by-case | Whether an area is in or out of RCHE GFA is decided case by case |

**SD takeaway:** Give RCHE its **own** lifts/stairs/corridor if you want those areas inside the concession GFA; shared-floor cores stay out — and RCHE-only plant/ducts sit **outside** APP-151’s 10% cap.

---

### Source

- `PN 5_2023.pdf`
- Read with: **LAO PN 4/2003** (base Incentive Scheme; **not applicable during Pilot**); SWD Guidance Note (June 2023); **PNAP APP-151**; Cap. **459**; TPO / BO
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 5-2023 (June 2023)_CS.md`

**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
18 June 2026 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Effective **20 June 2026** | Varies/supplements **LAO PN 5/2023**

> Scope note: **Continues** the PN 5/2023 enhancement measures after the three-year pilot ended **19 June 2026**. It does **not** change the design/GFA/cap rules in PN 5/2023. **LAO PN 4/2003** remains **not applicable** while these enhancements continue.

---

## Regulatory Overview

The 2003 Incentive Scheme (PN 4/2003) was enhanced for a pilot from **20 June 2023 to 19 June 2026** under PN 5/2023. Policy approval has been given for those enhancements to **continue**; from **20 June 2026**, LandsD applications under the Incentive Scheme are processed under **PN 5/2023 until further notice**, with existing arrangements and requirements **unchanged**.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. What this PN changes (paras 1–6)

| Item | Lock |
|---|---|
| Effective | **20 June 2026** |
| Parent | **PN 5/2023** — all enhancement arrangements/requirements **unchanged** |
| Pilot end | **19 June 2026** (does **not** lapse the enhancements) |
| Processing | Continue under **PN 5/2023** until Government further notice |
| PN 4/2003 | **Not applicable** while PN 5/2023 enhancements are continued by this PN |
| SWD | Refer to SWD website and SWD Guidance Note released **June 2026** |

**SD takeaway:** After 19 June 2026, still design to **PN 5/2023** (premium-free RCHE GFA cap, SWD location, whole-unit alienation) — do **not** fall back to PN 4/2003 unless the Government later says so.

<a id="pn-6-2023"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 6/2023 — Tree Preservation and Removal Proposals under Lease
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
20 June 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Generally revamped | **Supersedes** LAO PN **2/2020** and **2/2020A**  
SCC of TPRP (paras 10–11) effective **20 June 2023** for new TPRPs

> Scope note: Streamlined **Tree Preservation Clause** consent for building developments — TPRP inside/outside lot, deemed approval where TPB/PlanD already approved the landscape, and **self-certification**. It is **not** a GEO slope code. Read with LandsD **Guidance Notes** (flow charts and Specified Forms) on landsd.gov.hk. Standard clause: no tree on the lot or adjacent thereto shall be removed or interfered with without the Director’s prior written consent; consent may require transplanting, compensatory landscaping or replanting.

---

## Regulatory Overview

A **tree** is a plant with trunk diameter **≥ 95 mm at 1.3 m** above ground (AFCD Nature Conservation Practice Note No. 2 for DBH). AP and RLA must assess tree impact at planning/early design **in parallel with GBP** to BD (and treat existing trees as part of any Landscape Submission); unauthorised felling can block **presale consent** and **CC** and attract lease enforcement (BD site monitoring reports felling to LandsD).

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. TPRP within lot — three approval tracks (paras 4–12)

| Track | When | LandsD action |
|---|---|---|
| **Deemed approval** (paras 4–5) | TPRP is **part of** a Landscape Submission (LMP/LP) **already approved** by TPB/PlanD | Specified Form + entire TPRP in the approved LMP/LP + **RLA certificate** that it is that approved landscape. Acknowledgement within **10 WD** = deemed Prior Written Consent. After PlanD approves **completion** of landscape works: Deemed Compliance Submission → acknowledgement within **10 WD** |
| Deemed tracks **do not** apply | Lot boundary **substantially different** from the planning site; **or** B/D consultation needed — trees to be felled/interfered/replanted on land to be **handed back** under lease, or on a **Coloured Area** | Full vetting |
| **Vetted approval** (para 8) | TPRP **not** part of a planning Landscape Submission | Based on latest GBP, Foundation Plans, Site Formation (incl. ELS if required). Needs: no adverse LAT landscape advice; **no** removal/interference with **trees of particular value** designated under lease (OVT, potential OVT, rare species, stonewall trees, trees of particular interest, etc.); plus Guidance Notes para 2 criteria. Processing normally **≤ 8 weeks**. Conditions typically: implement Approved TPRP to LandsD’s satisfaction; **no tree removal before BD approval of Foundation and Site Formation (incl. ELS)** |
| **During a land transaction** (para 9) | Exchange / grant / modification **containing a Tree Preservation Clause** is in progress | After **BBTO accepted and deposit paid**, submit TPRP; formal approval **after** the transaction completes |
| **SCC of TPRP** (paras 10–13) | RLA certifies: no trees of particular value affected; no unnecessary removal/excessive pruning; best endeavours to compensate and greenery; **1:1** compensatory replanting **by number within the lot** (compensatory trees ≥ trees removed including dead, **excluding undesirable species**) | Random check. Not selected: acknowledgement within **10 WD** = deemed consent. Selected: result within **8 weeks**; fail → resubmit. **Does not apply** if comments needed from a future maintenance department (e.g. compensatory trees on land to be carved out/surrendered) or para 10 cannot be met |

**SD takeaway:** If TPB/PlanD already approved the LMP/LP, do **not** redesign the tree package for LandsD — file deemed approval. If using SCC, lock **1:1 replanting inside the lot** and keep **OVTs/rare/stonewall trees**. Do not fell before **foundation/site-formation** approval on the vetted track.

---

### 2. Completion, minor changes, outside-lot trees (paras 14–21)

| Stage | Lock |
|---|---|
| SCC of completion | RLA inspects and certifies approved tree works complete; random **on-site** check; not selected → deemed compliance (acknowledgement in **10 WD**); selected → result in **8 weeks**; rectification needs a consolidated completion resubmission |
| Minor changes to Approved TPRP | More trees preserved/planted; minor location/size/species changes **within lot** — **no** re-approval if LandsD agrees they are minor (LandsD’s call is **final**) |
| Supervision | Approved tree works by **Qualified Professionals** (DEVB Handbook on Tree Management App I) |
| Trees **outside lot** or on Coloured Area to be handed to another B/D (e.g. LCSD, HyD) | Submit TPRP to that **maintenance B/D first**; then Specified Form to DLO with the B/D’s agreement letter; LandsD acknowledgement within **10 WD** = Prior Written Consent; that B/D checks completion |
| More than one B/D | LandsD **coordinates**; **demarcate** hand-back areas on the TPRP |
| Coloured Area **retained** by the owner, or GL that LandsD will take over | Submit TPRP to LandsD; SCC of completion (paras 14–17) applies |
| Workshop | **LAO PN 4/2018** workshop may include TPRP vs building layout; LAT may attend |

**SD takeaway:** Off-lot and coloured-area trees are a **B/D** problem first (agreement letter, then 10 WD LandsD stamp) — draw a **demarcation** where two departments take over.

---

### 3. Emergency felling and enforcement (paras 23–27)

| Rule | Lock |
|---|---|
| Owner duty | Manage/maintain trees on the lot (and Coloured Area if the lease so requires), including horticultural maintenance and emergency felling/pruning for natural hazard/safety |
| Record | Full **colour, date-stamped** photos of ground, stem/branch/canopy/root-collar/root-zone/root-ball **before and after**; same-occasion photos acceptable for speed |
| Report | In writing to LandsD within **21 calendar days**, with reasons and photos; rely on qualified advice including RLA |
| If planning conditions exist | Also **resubmit updated LMP/LP to PlanD** |
| If no planning conditions | Cover in RLA’s TPRP re-submission/amendment with compensatory replanting |
| Breach | LandsD may **withhold presale consent or CC** until Director’s terms are met (including **premium, admin fee, compensatory replanting**). TPRP approval is **only** under the Tree Preservation Clause and does **not** prove other lease compliance; GBP can still be rejected |

**SD takeaway:** After typhoon/emergency works, diary the **21-day** LandsD report with before/after photos — and if there is a planning landscape condition, update **PlanD** as well, or CC/presale can be held.

---

### Source

- `PN 6_2023.pdf`
- Read with: LandsD **Guidance Notes on TPRP**; **PlanD PN 1/2019** (LMP/LP); **LAO PN 4/2018** (workshop); AFCD NCPN No. 2; DEVB TC(W) **6/2015**; DEVB **Handbook on Tree Management**
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 6-2023 (June 2023)_CS.md`

## LAO Practice Note No. 7/2023 — Access to Computerized "A" Rent Rolls Record
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
10 July 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Effective 10 July 2023 | Supersedes LAO PN Nos. 6/2006 and 6/2006A

> Scope note: Computerises New Territories **"A" Rent Rolls** ("A Book") at NT District Lands Offices and sets who may inspect them. It is **not** a BO/OZP development-control PN. Use it at SD only when you need NT lot identity, site area, user/class, or Government rent from the A Book — then verify at the Land Registry.

---

## Regulatory Overview

NTDLOs (Islands, North, Sai Kung, Sha Tin, Tai Po, Tsuen Wan and Kwai Tsing, Tuen Mun, Yuen Long) maintain the A Book for Government rent collection; all A Books are now electronic. From 10 July 2023, inspection of the original paper A Book ceases; electronic access is limited by the Personal Data (Privacy) Ordinance (Cap. 486).

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Who may inspect / what you get (paras 2–3)

| Who | Inspect electronic A Book | Printed report |
|---|---|---|
| Property owner or authorised person | **Free** — only properties under that owner's name, at the relevant NTDLO | Chargeable |
| Members of the public | **Free** — only lots **solely** owned by a deceased owner, a Tso/Tong, or a company, **or jointly owned by any of such parties** | Chargeable |

| Printed-report fields | Notes |
|---|---|
| Lot number; New Grant / Building Licence number | Identity |
| Site area; annual Government rent; class/user of the lot | SD lot facts |
| Land Registry memorial information | If available |
| Rent concession status | **Owner / authorised person only** |

Charge at issue: **$325 per property** (subject to revision). LandsD makes **no warranty** of accuracy; verify by Land Registry search / legal advice. A Book is internal LandsD use, not a public record.

**SD takeaway:** For an NT lot, treat A-Book site area / class / user as a lead only — freeze lease identity from the Land Grant and Land Registry, not from the A Book.

---

### 2. How to apply (para 4, Annexes A–D)

Submit the relevant form (Annex A, B or C) plus consent form Annex D where appropriate, to the **respective NTDLO**. Original paper A Book inspection by owners/authorised persons **stops** with this electronic service.

**SD takeaway:** Budget a NTDLO application (and $325/lot printout) if SD needs A-Book user/class; do not assume walk-in paper A Book access.

---

### Source

- `PN 7_2023.pdf` (LAO PN No. 7/2023, 10 July 2023)
- Read with: **Personal Data (Privacy) Ordinance (Cap. 486)**; Land Registry search. Supersedes **LAO PN 6/2006** and **6/2006A**.
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 7_2023 (July 2023)_CS.md`

## LAO Practice Note No. 8/2023 — Streamlined Requirement on Submission of Ownership and Land Grant Records
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
3 October 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Immediate effect | Supersedes LAO PN 1/2023 | Varies the Relevant PNs listed below

> Scope note: Cuts paper from **lease-mod / waiver / IB / MiC / NDA** application packs: drop Land Grant Document copies and historical ownership printouts; lodge **one plain current-ownership computer printout** only. It does **not** change the design tests in the parent PNs.

---

## Regulatory Overview

This PN streamlines ownership/land-grant evidence for applications under LAO PN Nos. 4/2005, 3/2012B, 1/2016, 1/2017, 2/2019, 3/2019, 4/2019, 5/2019, 6/2019, 1/2021, 1/2021A, 1/2022, 5/2022, 6/2022, 2/2023 and 3/2023 (the Relevant PNs). Except as varied here (Annex), those PNs remain valid. LAO PN 1/2023 is superseded.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. What to submit now (paras 2–3)

| Former requirement (dropped) | New requirement (immediate) |
|---|---|
| Complete set of Government lease / Conditions of Sale, Grant or Exchange, including executed modification and extension letters ("Land Grant Documents") | **No longer required** |
| Computer printout of **historical and current** ownership, often **certified** by Land Registry or the applicant's solicitor **within one month** before submission | **One plain copy** of a computer printout of **current** ownership particulars of the relevant property only (hard or soft copy per Annex Part B) |

**SD takeaway:** Application packs under the Relevant PNs no longer wait on certified Land Grant sets — attach a **current** ownership printout and proceed; LandsD already holds the lease.

---

### 2. Parent PNs amended (Annex Part A — design-facing list)

Annex Part B **2** (for PN 1/2017): the computer printout is submitted **at first submission of the building plans**.

## LAO Practice Note No. 9/2023 — Installation and Operation of Smart Lockers in Private Residential Developments
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
8 December 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department

> Scope note: LandsD will **not enforce** the residential user restriction against **public-use smart lockers in common areas** of a residential development if **all** criteria in para 2 are met. It is **not** a waiver of the BO, TPO, other lease conditions, or the DMC. Director's decision on "common area" and "residential block" is final.

---

## Regulatory Overview

On a lot restricted to residential user under lease, smart lockers accessible to the **general public** (not solely residents) would breach the user restriction. This PN sets a non-enforcement envelope for lockers in DMC common areas. "Residential user restriction" includes "private residential purposes", "residential purposes", "residence(s)", "as a dwelling house" and "as a messuage or dwelling house". "Lease" includes Government Lease or Conditions of Sale/Grant/Exchange.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Non-enforcement criteria — all must be met (paras 2–3)

| Criterion | Hard lock |
|---|---|
| **(a) Floor area cap** | Aggregate floor area of smart lockers **≤ 3 m² × number of residential blocks** in the development |
| **(b) Height** | Lockers **≤ 2.75 m** |
| **(c) Location** | **No** lockers in common areas designated for **public passage**, or in **MOE / emergency escape routes or exits**; installation must not contravene any other lease conditions |
| **(d) Signage** | No advertising sign, bill, placard or notice **except** name and contact of the locker provider and information **essential** for operation |
| **(e) Fixing** | Securely attached to the **floor or the wall** |

There is **no** cap on locker count **per block**, provided the development-wide aggregate area stays within para 2(a). Director's decision on what constitutes a residential block, and on whether an area is common area (generally IO/manager control, designated as such in the DMC), is final.

**SD takeaway:** Size the locker room (or alcove) to **≤ 3 m² per residential block**, keep it **≤ 2.75 m** high, off MOE and public-passage common parts, and show it as DMC common area — public-use lockers outside that envelope remain a user-clause breach.

---

### 2. Who must comply; what this PN does not touch (paras 4–7)

Incorporated Owners / OC or the property manager must ensure compliance with para 2, other lease conditions, **and the DMC**. Government accepts no liability for loss from lockers. The PN does not vary or waive the lease, and does not affect the BO, TPO, other legislation, or DMC rights.

**SD takeaway:** Even inside the LandsD envelope, check DMC common-parts use and BA MOE — LandsD non-enforcement is not a DMC or BO clearance.

---

### Source

- `PN 9_2023.pdf` (LAO PN No. 9/2023, 8 December 2023)
- Read with: the lot's **user clause** and **DMC** common-parts plan; **BO** MOE standards.
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 9_2023 (December 2023)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-10-2023"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 10/2023 — Incentive Scheme to Encourage Provision of Residential Care Homes for Persons with Disabilities in New Private Developments
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
20 December 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Pilot Period: **20 December 2023 – 19 December 2026**

> Scope note: Premium and (during the Pilot Period) **lease GFA** concessions for **SWD-supported RCHD** in **new** private developments. **Industrial** developments are excluded. Addition, alteration or conversion of **existing** buildings is **not** a new private development. RCHD remains **GFA-accountable under B(P)R**; BA may grant modification for higher PR/GFA during the Pilot Period.

---

## Regulatory Overview

A time-limited pilot (the Pilot Period) lets LandsD grant "Time-Limited Concessions" on applications received **during** that window; applications after 19 December 2026 are not considered for those concessions. After the Pilot Period, a smaller premium-only Concession remains. Eligibility requires **Social Welfare Department** support. Planning application under TPO s.12A or s.16 is required if RCHD is not always permitted and/or exceeds OZP PR/GFA/BH.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Quantum of concession (paras 2–4)

| Period | Premium | Lease GFA |
|---|---|---|
| **Pilot** (applications **20 Dec 2023 – 19 Dec 2026**) | Exempt **one or more** eligible RCHD premises, total GFA **≤ 12,000 m² or 10% of total permissible GFA under lease, whichever is the greater**, per development project, from land premium on lease modifications, land exchanges and PTGs ("Land Transactions") | **Exempt** eligible RCHD premises from **total permissible GFA under lease** |
| **After 19 Dec 2026** | Exempt **one** eligible RCHD premises, GFA **≤ 5,400 m²** per development project, from land premium | **No** lease-GFA exemption |

**SD takeaway:** To bank both premium **and** extra lease GFA, lodge the Land Transaction application **inside the Pilot Period** and size RCHD to **max(12,000 m², 10% of lease GFA)**; after the pilot you get premium relief only, cap **5,400 m²**, and RCHD eats the lease GFA budget.

---

### 2. Eligibility locks (paras 5–6)

| Condition | Lock |
|---|---|
| SWD | Size and **location** of RCHD premises approved by SWD |
| Alienation | **No sub-division**; no assignment, mortgage, underletting or other disposal **except as a whole unit** |
| Operation | Throughout the lease term, managed and operated as **licensed RCHD(s)** under the Residential Care Homes (Persons with Disabilities) Ordinance (**Cap. 613**) and subsidiary legislation, to SWD's satisfaction |
| Developer choice | Subject to SWD approval: **lease or sell** each completed RCHD as a whole unit, **or** self-operate |
| Vacancy / change of use | Unauthorised change of use, or vacant **> 12 months** as identified by SWD, may trigger lease enforcement including **re-entry** and Government taking possession **without compensation** |

These requirements are to be stipulated as lease conditions as appropriate. Also see SWD Guidance Note (December 2023).

**SD takeaway:** Plan RCHD as a **single undivided premises** (location fixed with SWD) with a 12-month vacancy tripwire — not a subdivisible commercial floor.

---

### 3. How RCHD GFA is measured (Annex)

GFA of eligible RCHD includes:

| Included | Excluded |
|---|---|
| Areas of RCHD(s) including **enclosing walls** and structures within the premises | Horizontal areas of stairs, lift shafts, lobbies and corridors **shared** with other uses on the same floor |
| Stairs, lift shafts, lobbies and corridors **solely** serving the RCHD(s), including their horizontal areas on the RCHD floor where they serve RCHD only | — |

| Remark | Design lock |
|---|---|
| Cap. 613 licensing | Enclosing walls and internal structures are **excluded** from floor space **per resident** (different from this GFA) |
| Plant / parking / LUL | May be disregarded under the prevailing GFA-concession mechanism, **except** remark (4) |
| **Non-mandatory / non-essential plant rooms** and horizontal areas of **vertical ducts solely serving the RCHD** | **Not** subject to the **10% overall cap** of GFA concession under **PNAP APP-151** |
| Authority | PlanD and LandsD rely on **BD's verified GFA** on the GBPs |

**SD takeaway:** Give RCHD **dedicated** stairs/lifts/corridor if you want that circulation inside the concession GFA; shared mall circulation stays outside RCHD GFA. Sole-serving non-mandatory plant is **outside** the APP-151 10% cap.

---

### Source

- `PN 10_2023.pdf` (LAO PN No. 10/2023, 20 December 2023)
- Read with: **Cap. 613**; **TPO** s.12A/s.16 and the OZP; **BO / B(P)R** (RCHD GFA-accountable; BA PR modification in the Pilot Period); **PNAP APP-151**; SWD Guidance Note (Dec 2023).
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 10_2023 (December 2023)_CS.md`

## LAO Practice Note No. 11/2023 — Pilot Scheme for Charging Land Premium at Standard Rates on Lease Modification including Land Exchange for Development of Agricultural Land in the NT outside NDAs
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
28 December 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Rates published separately (supplementary PN, first quarter 2024) | First rates review **no earlier than 1 April 2025**

> Scope note: Optional **standard-rate premium** for land exchange of **NT agricultural land outside NDAs**. It does **not** apply inside NDAs (KTN/FLN: **PN 3/2022** and **3/2022A**; other NDAs: PNs to follow) or inside **carved-out** strategic-study areas. Design GFA still comes from the OZP/lease — this PN only prices the exchange.

---

## Regulatory Overview

The Pilot Scheme extends standard-rate premium (as an alternative to case-by-case assessment) to development of agricultural land in the New Territories **outside** New Development Areas, by phases. Applicants may still choose conventional assessment. LandsD's decision on eligibility is final.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Eligibility and option timing (paras 2–3, 6)

| Test | Lock |
|---|---|
| Mix | **Not less than 50%** of the **site area of the private land under application** is agricultural land. That 50% is measured on the private land **proposed to be surrendered** |
| Agricultural land | Old schedule and new grant lots demised for agricultural use, **and** the agricultural portion of mixed lots (agriculture + building). May be whole lot, part, or multiple lots to match the OZP development-site boundary. LandsD determines areas from lease, land-boundary records and ground survey; **final** |
| Option | Opt **not later than acceptance of the Provisional Basic Terms Offer (PBTO)** on LandsD's specified option form (supplementary PN). Option is **irreversible** on PBTO acceptance — **no appeal** against standard-rate premium in the Binding Basic Terms Offer (BBTO). Fees **forfeited** if the applicant then walks away |
| Which rates | For each opted application, rates **prevailing at PBTO acceptance** apply |

**SD takeaway:** Do not opt unless **≥ 50% of surrendered site area** is agri land and you can live with an **unappealable** standard-rate BBTO.

---

### 2. Phase-1 geography (para 4, Appendix I)

First phase: **10 zones** in **Yuen Long, North and Tuen Mun** — four existing new towns and six railway catchments. LandsD's 1-km radius from a station (existing, or proposed with firm implementation commitment) is **final**. LandsD has absolute discretion whether a catchment is covered.

| No. | District | Zone |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Yuen Long | Yuen Long Town |
| 2 | Yuen Long | Hung Shui Kiu Station |
| 3 | Yuen Long | Kam Sheung Road Station |
| 4 | Yuen Long | Tin Shui Wai Town |
| 5 | Yuen Long | San Tin Station |
| 6 | Yuen Long | Ngau Tam Mei Station |
| 7 | Yuen Long | Au Tau Station |
| 8 | North | Fanling / Sheung Shui Town |
| 9 | North | Kwu Tung Station |
| 10(a)(b) | Tuen Mun | Tuen Mun Town **Sub-zone 1** / **Sub-zone 2** |

| Overlay rule | Lock |
|---|---|
| **Carved-out** | NDAs and areas under on-going strategic development studies (Appendix I note) — Option **not applicable**. LandsD decision final. Indicative carve-outs on the plan include KTN NDA, FLN NDA, Hung Shui Kiu / Ha Tsuen NDA, San Tin Technopole, NTN New Town, Ngau Tam Mei LUR, Ma Tso Lung, Tsim Bei Tsui / Lau Fau Shan / Pak Nai, Tuen Mun East / GB, Tuen Mun West / Lung Kwu Tan |
| Overlapping zones | **Higher** rates apply |
| Site partly in / partly out of coverage | **Whole site** eligible; LandsD decides rates for the part **outside** coverage |
| Site straddling **two zones or two sub-zones** | After-exchange **GFA** split **pro-rata by site area** in each zone/sub-zone, assuming GFA evenly distributed |

**SD takeaway:** Confirm the lot is **outside** NDA/study carve-outs and which of the 10 zones (or TM sub-zones) it sits in **before** SD GFA mix — straddling sites get **pro-rata** after-GFA rates, overlaps take the **higher** rate.

---

### Source

- `PN 11_2023.pdf` (LAO PN No. 11/2023, 28 December 2023)
- Read with: supplementary rates PN (**11/2023A**); **PN 3/2022** and **3/2022A** (KTN/FLN NDA standard rates — out of scope here). Map: Appendix I (LIC-MIS-2017n, 20/12/2023).
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 11_2023 (December 2023)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-11-2023a"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 11/2023A — Standard Rates for the Agricultural-Land Pilot Scheme (supplements PN 11/2023)
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
2 April 2024 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Rates effective **1 April 2024** | Supplements LAO PN No. 11/2023

> Scope note: Publishes the **standard rates** and the **A×B − C×D − E** formula for PN 11/2023. If the exchange also involves eligible **pre-1987 IBs** under **PN 12/2023**, the applicant may opt **only** these rates — **not** other PNs' rates. Does not restate PN 11/2023 geography except the overlap/pro-rata rules.

---

## Regulatory Overview

From 1 April 2024, standard rates for land exchange of NT agricultural land outside NDAs follow the zone table in paragraph 2, using the zones in paragraph 4 of PN 11/2023. Premium at standard rates is **not negotiable**. Negative calculated premium ⇒ **no premium charged**.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Rates by zone (para 2)

All rates **per GFA** except **Agricultural Use**, which is **per site area**.

| Zone | Before: Agri (site) | Before: Non-res | Before: Residential | After: Non-res | After: Residential |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yuen Long Town | $4,500/m² | $13,500/m² | $26,500/m² | $24,000/m² | $34,000/m² |
| Hung Shui Kiu Station | $4,500/m² | $13,500/m² | $26,500/m² | $24,000/m² | $34,000/m² |
| Kam Sheung Road Station | $4,500/m² | $13,500/m² | $26,500/m² | $20,000/m² | $32,000/m² |
| Tin Shui Wai Town | $4,500/m² | $13,500/m² | $26,500/m² | $20,000/m² | $32,000/m² |
| San Tin Station | $4,500/m² | $10,000/m² | $20,000/m² | $18,500/m² | $25,500/m² |
| Ngau Tam Mei Station | $4,500/m² | $10,000/m² | $20,000/m² | $18,500/m² | $25,500/m² |
| Au Tau Station | $4,500/m² | $10,500/m² | $21,000/m² | $20,000/m² | $27,000/m² |
| Fanling / Sheung Shui Town | $4,500/m² | $13,500/m² | $26,500/m² | $24,000/m² | $34,000/m² |
| Kwu Tung Station | $4,500/m² | $13,500/m² | $26,500/m² | $24,000/m² | $34,000/m² |
| Tuen Mun Town Sub-zone 1 | $4,500/m² | $13,500/m² | $26,500/m² | $20,000/m² | $32,000/m² |
| Tuen Mun Town Sub-zone 2 | $4,500/m² | $10,500/m² | $21,000/m² | $16,000/m² | $25,500/m² |

| Use label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| After: **residential** rate | All GFA **not prohibiting private residential use** |
| After: **non-residential** rate | Remainder GFA. Includes commercial and **modern industrial**; **excludes** columbarium, petrol filling station, storage of dangerous goods, offensive trades (standard rates **not applicable**). **Hotel and ancillary = non-residential** |
| Before: **non-residential** | Includes commercial and industrial. LandsD's classification is final |

Overlapping zones: **higher** rates. Straddle two zones/sub-zones: after-GFA **pro-rata by site area**, GFA assumed evenly distributed.

**SD takeaway:** Price the scheme as **after-GFA × after-rate** minus **surrendered agri m² × $4,500** (and any building-land GFA × before-rate) — hotel counts as **non-res**; PFS / DG / OT / columbarium knock you out of standard rates.

---

### 2. Formula (para 3)

**Premium = A × B − C × D − E**

| Factor | What LandsD uses |
|---|---|
| **A** | Maximum **total GFA after** the exchange as stipulated in the land exchange document(s). **GA** floor space may be disregarded. If GA is stated as **NOFA**, convert **NOFA:GFA = 1:1.8**. Other floor space disregarded **only** if **expressly** GFA-exempted in the document. **If no maximum total GFA is stipulated, this PN does not apply** |
| **B** | After-use standard rate(s) for the lot's zone(s) |
| **C** | Total **site area of Agricultural land** surrendered **and/or** total **permissible GFA of Building land** surrendered |
| **D** | Before-use standard rate(s). Building land: residential rate if GFA does not prohibit residential; else non-res |
| **E** | Deductible **construction cost of Government Accommodation** the applicant must build, assign/hand back, or O&M for public use — assessed by the proponent department and, if practicable, told to the applicant **before** the Option deadline. If not conveyed, **E = 0** |

**Building land (fn 9):** land on which building structure(s) is allowed, including the building portion of mixed lots. Old schedule lots are Building land (residential — residential not prohibited) if the Block Government Lease Schedule term is one or more of: house, building, mat shed, boat hut, temple, nunnery, latrine, shop, cow shed, cattle shed, duck shed, cow byre, pigsty, school, or other uses LandsD determines. Permissible GFA = GFA specified in the lease; **if none, plot ratio 1.33**. Untraceable leases: use and site area at LandsD discretion.

**SD takeaway:** Put a **maximum total GFA** (and GA as NOFA if needed) in the exchange document or the standard-rate option dies; old-schedule "house" lots credit **PR 1.33** residential GFA as "C".

---

### 3. Survey pack and transitional option (paras 4–6)

| When | What |
|---|---|
| To fix "C" | **Survey Submission** by an **Authorised Land Surveyor** (Cap. 473): surveyed/registered areas and boundaries of lots wholly surrendered; proposed areas/boundaries of partial surrenders; proposed regrant site. Plus **title deeds** of all lots to be surrendered |
| Deadline for that pack | Not later than settlement of **initial administrative fee**, or **two months from 2 April 2024** for applications already accepted for further processing |
| If not provided and applicant still opts standard rates | LandsD fixes "C" from information known to it and issues BBTO |
| Transitional (PBTO already accepted or BBTO issued but premium **not** accepted as of this PN) | One-time Option Form (Appendix I) by **31 May 2024**; missing form ⇒ deemed case-by-case. **Does not** apply if BBTO (including premium) **already accepted** |
| After PBTO accepted, want standard rates later | **Withdraw and resubmit** the land exchange (fresh fees/timeline). Walking away from a case-by-case BBTO **forfeits** fees |

**SD takeaway:** Commission the ALS boundary survey **before** PBTO if you want a known "C"; on-going cases had a **31 May 2024** one-shot option.

---

### Source

- `PN 11_2023A.pdf` (LAO PN No. 11/2023A, 2 April 2024)
- Read with: **LAO PN 11/2023** (eligibility and zones); **LAO PN 12/2023** (if pre-1987 IB on the lot — these rates only). Option Form: Appendix I.
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 11_2023A (April 2024)_CS.md`

## LAO Practice Note No. 12/2023 — Charging Land Premium at Standard Rates on Lease Modifications for Redevelopment of Industrial Buildings
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
28 December 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Immediate effect | Supersedes LAO PN Nos. 1/2021 and 1/2021A | These rates valid **until 31 March 2024** (later rates to be promulgated)

> Scope note: Optional **standard-rate premium** for lease modification / in-situ land exchange to **redevelop pre-1987 IBs** on industrial lots for **commercial, modern industrial and/or residential** use. **Not** for special uses (e.g. columbarium). IBs that completed **wholesale conversion** under the 2010 or 2018 IB revitalisation schemes are **not** pre-1987 IBs for this PN. **PN 2/2019** extra PR is **not** a prerequisite.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Applicants may stay on case-by-case premium or opt for standard rates **not later than PBTO acceptance** (Option Form, Appendix I). The Option is **irreversible**; no appeal against the BBTO premium; fees forfeited if the applicant then walks away. If no **maximum total GFA** is stipulated in the modification document, the scheme **does not apply**. Straightforward BBTO: normally **within five weeks** of PBTO acceptance.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. Eligible stock (paras 1–3, fn 1–4)

| Term | Lock |
|---|---|
| **Industrial building** | Built for industrial and/or godown use on an industrial lot |
| **Industrial lot** | Land grant shall not be used other than for industrial or godown purposes or both. **Industrial** includes special industrial (leather tanning, garment manufacturing, food production, etc.). A lot **without** a specific user restriction is also an industrial lot for this PN. **Vacant** industrial lots that were occupied by pre-1987 IBs immediately before vacancy **are** eligible |
| **Pre-1987 IB** | Wholly or partly constructed **on or before 1 March 1987**, **or** constructed to building plans **first submitted** to BA **on or before** 1 March 1987 |
| **Not eligible as pre-1987** | IBs that completed wholesale conversion under the **2010 or 2018** IB Revitalisation Schemes |
| After-use | Commercial, modern industrial and/or residential. **Hotel and hotel ancillary GFA = commercial**. LandsD decides what is "special use" outside the scheme |

If the lot falls in an **NDA** or in the **agricultural-land pilot** areas, the applicant may opt **only** the standard rates in **those** PNs, not this PN.

**SD takeaway:** Confirm OP or first GBP submission **on/before 1 March 1987**, and that the IB was **not** wholesale-converted under 2010/2018 schemes, before banking this premium path.

---

### 2. Formula (para 5)

**Premium = A × B − C × D − E** (negative ⇒ no premium).

| Factor | Lock |
|---|---|
| **A** | Maximum total GFA after modification. **GA** may be disregarded; GA stated as NOFA ⇒ **NOFA:GFA = 1:1.8**. **Public Vehicle Park is GFA-accountable** unless the modification document exempts it. Only floor space **expressly** GFA-exempted in the document is otherwise disregarded |
| **B** | After-use rate for the region. **Residential** rates apply to all applications **not prohibiting private residential**, **except** sites in OZP **"CDA"** or **"OU"** for commercial-cum-residential (and/or related planning approvals): residential rate on GFA not prohibiting private residential; **commercial/modern industrial** rate on the remainder |
| **C** | GFA of the pre-1987 IB existing on the lot (or existing immediately before vacancy) per **latest BA-approved building plans**, **excluding** parking, L/UL, plant rooms and similar facilities **not normally GFA-accountable under the BO**, and any GFA on those plans that **does not comply with the lease**. Certify by **AP** or **Registered Professional Surveyor**. Alternative (irreversible): GFA as if redeveloped for industrial/godown **before** modification, as LandsD would use in a case-by-case assessment |
| **D** | Before-use rate: Industrial/Godown (except Special Industrial) — also where the grant has **no** user restriction — or **Special Industrial**, by region |
| **E** | GA construction cost (same logic as other standard-rate PNs); **E = 0** if not conveyed before the Option deadline |

**SD takeaway:** Freeze **max GFA after** (PVP counts unless exempted) and AP-certify existing IB GFA **net of** non-accountable parking/plant; CDA/OU mixed sites split **B** between residential and commercial rates.

---

### 3. Rates to 31 March 2024 (para 6, Appendix II)

| Region (Appendix II) | DLO mapping | Before: I/G (except Special Ind) | Before: Special Ind | After: Comm / Modern Ind | After: Residential |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hong Kong Island | HK West & South, HK East | $40,000/m² | $28,000/m² | $75,000/m² | $130,000/m² |
| Kowloon East | Kowloon East | $40,000/m² | $28,000/m² | $65,000/m² | $100,000/m² |
| Kowloon West | Kowloon West | $35,000/m² | $24,500/m² | $60,000/m² | $110,000/m² |
| New Territories South | Tsuen Wan & Kwai Tsing, Sha Tin, Sai Kung | $35,000/m² | $24,500/m² | $50,000/m² | $75,000/m² |
| New Territories North | North, Tai Po, Tuen Mun, Yuen Long, Islands | $20,000/m² | $14,000/m² | $30,000/m² | $55,000/m² |

Rates applying are those **prevailing at PBTO acceptance**. Building covenant on the modification is set by complexity, normally running from **execution**.

**SD takeaway:** Use the five-region map (LIC-MIS-196c, 29/11/2023) — **NT North is half or less** of HK Island after-rates — and remember these tabulated figures expired **31 March 2024** unless a later PN has replaced them.

---

### 4. Enquiry Form and transitional (paras 7, 10–11)

| Tool | When |
|---|---|
| Enquiry Form (Appendix III) + supporting docs | Not later than settlement of **initial administrative fee** → LandsD states "C" with the PBTO |
| Transitional for **special industrial** lots with PBTO already accepted or BBTO issued but premium **not** accepted as of this PN | Opt by **28 February 2024**; Enquiry Form by **26 January 2024** if "C" needed first. **Not** if BBTO including premium already accepted |
| Opt after PBTO but before BBTO | **Resubmit** the lease modification (fresh fees/timeline) |

Option Form must include OP **or** BA letter showing first plan submission on/before 1 March 1987, plus the AP/RPS GFA Certificate and approved plans (unless using the alternative "C" basis).

**SD takeaway:** Lodge the Enquiry Form with the AP GFA certificate **with the admin-fee payment** so "C" is known before you irreversibly opt.

---

### Source

- `PN 12_2023.pdf` (LAO PN No. 12/2023, 28 December 2023; first issued March 2021 as PN 1/2021, last revised March 2022 as 1/2021A — both superseded)
- Read with: **PN 11/2023** / NDA standard-rate PNs (those rates override if the lot is in those areas); **PN 2/2019** (optional extra PR, not required). Forms: Appendices I and III; regions: Appendix II.
*Source Critical Summary:* `source_md/LAO Practice Note No. 12_2023 (December 2023)_CS.md`

<a id="pn-13-2023"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 13/2023 — New Arrangements for Applications for Lease Modification including Land Exchange under the Enhanced Conventional New Town Approach
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
28 December 2023 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Immediate effect for designated-site broadening, 90% ownership option, and occupant Government-package option

> Scope note: Application rules for **ECNTA land exchange** in **all NDAs** — not a GFA/BO code. Sites must be **Government-designated** for private development. If exchange fails, Government resumes and disposes (e.g. public tender). Standard-rate premium is offered as an option; rates come in **separate PNs** per NDA.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Under ECNTA the Government generally resumes all private land in an NDA, but before resumption it allows lease modification including land exchange from lot owners of **designated development sites** if prescribed criteria are met. This PN extends ECNTA beyond KTN/FLN to **all NDAs** and sets general application requirements. LandsD acting as landlord may approve or reject any application.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. What sites and uses (paras 3–6, Appendix I)

| Lock | Detail |
|---|---|
| Where | Only **designated development sites** earmarked for private developments in an NDA, announced by LandsD (plans on LandsD website). Timing varies by NDA |
| Uses now included | Not only **residential and commercial** — also **industries** and **privately-run community or welfare facilities**, as Government considers appropriate |
| After-use | As permitted under the statutory town plan(s) or TPB, or as advised by Government |
| Premium | Standard rates offered as an option (announced March 2022); **applicable rates per NDA** via separate PNs when applications are invited |
| Public facilities | Under certain circumstances (including the **90% ownership** option below), the grant may require the applicant to **construct public facilities** in the NDA (e.g. PTI, roads, open space, welfare/recreational). Construction cost **may** be premium-deductible if Government so decides. Standards and cost: relevant departments, **final** |
| Publication | LandsD publishes accepted applications (including a plan of the private lots); occupants are notified |

Appendix I general criteria: location inside a designated site; **no** surrender of lots **outside** the designated site (normally); **minimum size** (varies by site/NDA); single owner or joint venture; reasonably **regular**, **contiguous** (fragmented lots not accepted); OZP/ODP compliance; **proper vehicular access** feasible; no insurmountable planning/layout/urban-design harm and no jeopardy to adjacent lots' development potential or access. Applications meeting only the **minimum size** must submit a **layout plan** showing decent, practical development **fully utilising** the designated site **without loss of planned development potential**.

**SD takeaway:** Design to the **full designated-site** envelope (not just lots you own) and prove access and no leftover undevelopable fragments — a minimum-size application needs a layout that consumes the **whole** designated site.

---

### 2. Relaxed 90% ownership and 2-stage grant (paras 7–10)

| Item | Lock |
|---|---|
| Default | Minimum surrendered size (Appendix I para 3) plus other criteria |
| **Relaxed ownership** | If the applicant has acquired **≥ 90%** of **all private land** within a designated development site, may opt for Government to **resume the remaining private land** and grant the **entire** designated site (Government's absolute discretion) |
| Price of that option | Applicant **must construct public facilities** in the NDA (para 6) |
| Stage one | Surrender applicant's private land (+ Government land if any) → **Initial Land Grant** of a new lot; pay **Initial Premium** = pro-rata current market value of the **whole** designated site at BBTO. On accepting that BBTO, sign an Agreement to take up the remainder after resumption, and pay an **upfront deposit** = difference between whole-site CMV and Initial Premium |
| Stage two | After resumption, grant of resumed land **or** surrender of the Initial lot for a **Complete Land Grant**; **Top-Up Premium** or **partial refund** of deposit (no interest, within **30 days** of Complete Land Grant completion) to reflect the resumed land's pro-rata CMV at that BBTO. Top-up payable on accepting the Complete Land Grant BBTO |

**SD takeaway:** At **≥ 90%** assembled area you can design the **entire** designated site, but programme a **two-title** grant, an upfront deposit, and **public facilities** as a lease obligation.

---

### 3. Occupants, vacant possession, time limit (paras 11–15, Appendix II)

| Requirement | Lock |
|---|---|
| Comparable package | All eligible occupants (occupying as at LandsD **pre-clearance survey** or thereafter) must accept applicant offers **comparable** to Government NDA EGA (cash) or **non-means-tested rehousing** (in kind or cash-equivalent). Applicant's package is **not** premium-deductible |
| Occupant may choose Government | Eligible occupants may opt for **Government** compensation/rehousing. Applicant **reimburses** Government on a cost-recovery basis: compensation paid and/or cash value of rehousing based on **market value of land + construction cost of a comparable unit**, plus admin cost. Costing basis: a **non-means-tested subsidised sale flat in a dedicated rehousing estate**, **regardless** of the occupant's actual choice. Reimburse **with the land premium** |
| Before BBTO | Prove acceptances **or** give a LandsD-satisfactory **Undertaking** per hold-out occupant: keep offers open **3 years from execution**; reimburse if they later take Government's package; **deposit** with Government an amount equal to those Government costs. Deposit deductible if they accept Government's package (including **after** execution, within 3 years) |
| Release of Undertaking/deposit (no interest) | Occupant accepted applicant's comparable offer; **or** Government package fully reimbursed; **or** **3 years** from execution |
| Vacant possession | Documentary evidence that actual vacant possession **has been or will be** secured by a reasonable timing, before BBTO. Lots to be surrendered **free from encumbrances** before execution |
| PBTO is not enough | Exchange **not processed further** after PBTO until para 13 is met |
| Deadlines | Application-submission deadline **and** BBTO-acceptance deadline set by reference to Government's **resumption programme** — **final, non-negotiable**. Late applications not considered. If BBTO (with premium) cannot be issued and accepted by the deadline, processing **ceases**; admin fee **not refunded** |

Appendix II also: Government land adjoining/intervening the private land is included in the regrant only if **incapable of reasonable separate alienation or development** — Government will **not** normally add separately alienable GL just to fill the designated-site boundary.

**SD takeaway:** Programme **vacant possession and occupant settlements before BBTO**, and treat the NDA **resumption date** as a hard stop on the exchange — leftover Government land inside the designated site may **not** be in your lot.

---

### Source

- `PN 13_2023.pdf` (LAO PN No. 13/2023, 28 December 2023)
- Read with: NDA-specific invitation PNs (sites, rates, deadlines); **PN 10/2025** (premium offsetting with other agri land, where applicable). Criteria: Appendices I–II.

<a id="pn-1-2024"></a>

## LAO Practice Note No. 1/2024 — HSK/HT NDA land exchange under ECNTA
**Architect critical summary for schematic design**  
February 2024 | Lands Administration Office — Lands Department  
Read with **LAO PN No. 13/2023** (Enhanced Conventional New Town Approach)

> Scope note: Lease modification / in-situ land exchange for **designated development sites** in Hung Shui Kiu/Ha Tsuen NDA. It is **not** an OZP; **PN 12/2023** IB standard rates **do not apply**. Standard-rate premium for this NDA is “to be announced”.

---

## Regulatory Overview

Sites now open are those coloured yellow hatched red or green hatched red on the LandsD Reference Plan (Second Phase and Remaining Phase private residential, mixed R/C, commercial, or OU “Port Back-up, Storage and Workshop Uses”). Follow approved OZP **S/HSK/2** (26 October 2018 or later amendments) and the adopted HSK/HT ODP. Second Phase applications have priority; Remaining Phase is lower priority pending infrastructure/access.

---

## Critical main topics and subtopics

### 1. What is (and is not) open (para 3 and fn 1)

| Open | Closed (this stage) |
|---|---|
| Designated sites on the Reference Plan (illustration only; Government may amalgamate adjoining designated sites) | Sites with adjoining/intervening GL capable of **reasonable separate alienation**; Remaining Phase sites **under planning review**; sites affected by Government works; **R+P** railway sites; other policy exclusions |

**SD takeaway:** Confirm the lot sits inside a hatched designated site on the **current** LandsD Reference Plan (updated again by PN 1/2024A) before drawing a re-grant boundary.

---

### 2. Urban design and public facilities (paras 5–6)

| Item | Lock |
|---|---|
| Urban / green design | CEDD **Urban and Green Design Requirement Brief** and PlanD **Urban Design Guidelines** for Future Town Centre & District Commercial Node (hskhtnda.hk); may be written into the land grant |
| Public facilities | May be required (PTI, open space, welfare/recreation, etc.). **Relaxed 90% ownership** applicants **shall** construct public facilities; others **may** be invited. Specs and possible **premium-deductible** cost set per site |
| Planning Area **32A** (ODP) | Potential **strategic Transport Interchange Hub** facilities |

**SD takeaway:** Budget podium/site for Government-specified public facilities (and Area 32A hub) as a lease condition — they can be premium-deductible but will lock layout.

---

### 3. OU Port Back-up / Storage / Workshop sites marked “#” (para 7)

| Item | Lock |
|---|---|
| On completion | Hand over **no less than 30% of floor space** as **Government Accommodation** for displaced brownfield operators |
| Remainder | ~70% private; GA uses “generally compatible” but **not necessarily** the same as the 70% |
| Letting | Government takes over and leases to displaced operators on Government terms |

**SD takeaway:** On hashed OU workshop sites, freeze **≥ 30% GFA as GA** with independent access/services — GPA-style GA package, not a normal industrial floor.

---

### 4. Deadlines in this PN (para 9) — later varied by PN 1/2024A for some commercial sites

| Item | Date |
|---|---|
| Submit Second Phase applications | **30 April 2024** (late applications not considered) |
| Accepted Second Phase: BBTO with premium issued **and** accepted | **31 March 2025** (else processing ceases; fees not refunded) |
| Remaining Phase receive/conclude dates | To be announced on LandsD website |
| Lodge at | CES/NDA, Unit 1501-10, 15/F Landmark North, Sheung Shui |

**SD takeaway:** Second Phase design must be offer-ready by the BBTO date (31 March 2025 here; edged-red commercial sites later got 31 December 2025 under PN 1/2024A).

---

### 5. Specific criteria — Appendix III (must-submit design facts)

| Criterion | Lock |
|---|---|
| Confinement | Surrender **only** lots inside the designated site; outside lots not normally accepted |
| Commercial / OU C&R / OU Port Back-up sites | Surrender **all** private land in the designated site (minor boundary tweaks that do not hurt layout OK). Planning intention = **one development per designated site**. **90%+** ownership → may use PN 13/2023 relaxed criterion. Re-grant to **conform to designated site** (GL not promised) |
| **Residential** sites | Surrender ≥ **4 000 m²**. 90% + 4 000 m² → relaxed criterion available. Site **contiguous**, reasonably regular, no intervening lots except residual lots under the relaxed criterion. If using **4 000 m² only** without full-site assembly: **layout plans** proving decent full-site utilisation |
| Access | Proper **vehicular access** feasible |
| OZP | Use and parameters **comply** |
| No adverse implications | Must not block adjacent lots’ potential or access |

**SD takeaway:** Commercial/OU designated sites are **all-or-90%** assemblies; residential can be 4 000 m² but then prove the leftover of the designated site still works.

---

### 6. Submission pack (App I) — design-facing

Must: application letter with proposed user, **max GFA, SC, storeys/BH**; 1:1000 site plan; TPB approval if any; ownership printout.

Facilitate: OZP extract; assignment/Deed Poll plans for portions; **sketch plans**; approved MLP / EIA / TIA / DIA; occupant schedules (existing **and** occupants at **13 April 2017** or after who later left); development programme; ALS plan-area/boundary survey to prove the **90%** private-land threshold.

**SD takeaway:** Put GFA/SC/BH, 90% survey, 13 April 2017 occupant trail, and (for partial residential sites) full-site layouts in the first pack.

---

- `PN 1_2024.pdf`
